2  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/
上週重要市場資訊
 隨著法國距離下屆總統大選僅剩六個月，極左翼與政府間的政治對立升溫，然而
法國政府急需推動預算案以控制財政赤字與龐大債務。在內政危機加劇與工會
預告 11月大罷工的背景下，市場對法國債務可持續性的擔憂升溫，將帶動法德
公債利差維持高檔，進而推升歐元區主權風險溢酬並拖累歐元表現。
 上週路透最新調查顯示，在 100 位受訪經濟學家中，高達 94%預期聯準會將於
10 月會議維持利率在 3.75%-4.00% 不變，惟全員一致預估 12 月將重啟升息。
儘管美國受 AI 投資熱潮帶動、經濟成長強勁，且美伊戰事推升油價突破百元
大關、加徵關稅使 PCE 通膨至少在 2028 年前難以回到 2% 目標，官員仍傾
向審慎行事。不過逾四成專家警告，聯準會恐落後通膨形勢，後續面臨至少再升
息兩次甚至擴大升息幅度的上行風險，預期美元大幅回檔的空間小，逢低承接買
盤依然強勁。
 路透最新調查顯示，受日圓疲軟與中東戰事推升能源及進口成本、加劇通膨壓力

影響，近七成受訪經濟學家預計日本央行將於 12月再度升息至 1.50%，延續 6
月與 9 月以來的較快緊縮步伐；同時，逾半數專家預估終端利率將上看 2.00%
或更高。不過，高達近四分之三受訪者警告，若高市早苗內閣提名的鴿派委員再
次投下反對票，恐壓抑日圓表現。在歐美央行年底前亦可能升息的背景下，日本
央行維持較快的緊縮節奏，有助於防止美日、歐日利差進一步擴大，避免日圓在
年底遭遇新一輪劇烈拋售，為日圓提供實質的下檔保護。

### Page 3

3  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
 美元兌新台幣匯率走勢  
Oct/27/2026 
＜上週走勢＞ 
    上週台幣匯價高點為 32.360，低點在 32.704，以 32.508作收，較前一周收盤價
貶值 1 角 7 分。 
上週台幣走貶，周初，外資延續前周大幅匯出的走勢，台幣跳空開低，跌破今
年 7 月的低點 32.528；隨著台股表現不佳，外資賣超台股並匯出，台幣續創低位，
最低來到 32.704周內低點；而後台股低檔反彈，外資由匯出轉為雙向進出，加以出
口商積極進場拋售美金，台幣由貶轉升至 32.500下方；臨近周末，市場擔憂颱風來
襲周五可能休市，增添連續假期變數，外資再度大幅賣超台股並匯出，令台幣又跌
回 32.600上方，終場接在央行調節之下收在 32.508。 
＜未來分析＞ 
    預估本週台幣走勢偏弱。近期全球科技股因 AI 類股估值偏高及融資風險疑慮
而持續修正，市場風險偏好降溫，股市面臨修正，外資連續賣超台股並匯出，台幣
走勢持續面臨壓力。另一方面，美伊戰事和談進展的消息反覆，令國際油價節節攀
升，突破每桶 110美元上方，引發通膨升溫的擔憂，支撐利率維持在高檔的預期，
美元指數也在 101.5 上方高位震盪，將不利台幣的表現。盤勢上來看，外資隨著股
市回檔修正表現偏向賣超台股並匯出，台幣偏弱，不過本周適逢月底，預計匯價逢
高仍持續有出口商拋售美金的需求，將限制台幣走貶的幅度。綜合以上，預估本週
台幣走勢偏弱。

＜預估本週＞ 美元兌台幣：32.300-32.800

### Page 4

4  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
美元兌人民幣匯率走勢  
Oct/27/2026 
＜上週走勢＞ 
上週人民幣走升。周初中國公布9月工業生產物價指數 5.5%，表現優於預期的
5.4%(前值 5.2%)，且第三季 GDP表現 4.5%，也優於預期的 4.4%(前值 4.3%)，基本
面穩健支撐人民幣升破 6.6800 整數關口；不過周間美元指數持續於 101.5 上方高位
震盪，加以美伊戰事未見緩解，國際油價持續飆升，突破每桶 110 美元上方，人民
幣於 6.6800下方高檔震盪；臨近周末，人行中間價格連續走升，升破6.7200整數關
口，週五最高來到 6.7180，顯示中國人行允許人民幣溫和升值的走勢，終場境外人
民幣升破 6.6700整數關口 ， 收在6.6690， 週線升值160點 ， 境內人民幣則收在6.6704，
週線升值 166 點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
     預估本週人民幣偏升。就中國國內市場來看，人行中間價持續偏升，將持續帶
動人民幣走升，加以出口商實質拋匯需求將支撐人民幣表現；就國際市場來看，最
新利率期貨顯示 ， 本周聯準會利率決議有9成的機率維持利率在 3.75%~4.00%不變 ，
不過年底前仍有一碼的升息空間，加以地緣政治風險未見緩解，能源價格高檔震盪
推升通膨與利率預期，美元指數維持在 101.5 上方高檔震盪，將限制人民幣升值的
速度跟幅度；最後就技術面來看，USD/CNH日線圖中，各天期均線向下發散，匯價
維持在五日均線之下交投，均線呈現空頭排列，顯示空方勢力較盛，綜合以上，預
估本週人民幣偏升。

＜預估本週＞ 美元兌人民幣：6.6300-6.6900

### Page 5

5  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
美元兌日圓匯率走勢  
         Oct/27/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
    上週 USD/JPY 震盪下跌。週初，USD/JPY 大致在 158 的整數關卡上下波動；
週間，AI相關概念股遭拋售，加之中東局勢緊張推升避險需求，10年期美債殖利率
跌破 5%，避險資金亦流入日圓，帶動 USD/JPY 下行最低來至 156.67；臨近週末，
市場避險需求持續加之日本 9 月通膨數據高於日本央行 2%目標，USD/JPY 一度下
跌至 156.06，後逢低買盤進場跌幅有所收斂，終場收在 156.86，週線下跌 113點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
  預估本週 USD/JPY區間震盪，主要原因如下： 一、美國10 月 S&P製造業 PMI
為 56.0高於 9 月的 55.9且為 53個月高點，服務業則為 57.9低於 9月的 58.8，綜合
PMI為 57.6亦低於 9 月的 58.4，顯示美國商業活動雖略有放緩但仍處於擴張，經濟
基本面依然穩健將持續支撐美元；此外，中東局勢惡化使能源供應風險上升，布蘭
特原油價格突破 116美元，為六個月以來高點，也將有利USD/JPY上行。二、日本
9 月全國整體消費者物價指數（CPI）年增 2.1%，且 9 月核心 CPI年增 2.1%，為九
個月以來首次超過日本央行 2%的目標，強化市場對日本央行短期升息的預期，預計
為日圓帶來支撐。三、就技術面而言，日線圖中，USD/JPY匯價處於各天期均線之
下且接連跌破 158 及 157 關卡，後在 156 整數關卡暫時獲得支撐，顯示逢低仍有買
盤進場，將限縮下行幅度。綜合以上，預估USD/JPY本週呈現區間震盪的可能性較
高。 
＜預估本週＞ 美元兌日幣：155.00-158.50 
前週交易區間 
156.06 158.59

6  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
美元兌歐元匯率走勢  
         Oct/27/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
    上週 EUR/USD震盪下跌。週初，EUR/USD在 1.1200整數關卡窄幅波動；週間
受到油價及美債殖利率回落，外加法國社會局勢略見緩解影響，帶動匯價反彈至
1.1260遇到賣壓後回落，後續大致在1.1190至 1.1250間波動；臨近週末，歐元區10
月份 PMI表現低迷，且市場對於法國政局和財政赤字擔憂持續，拖累 EUR/USD轉
若，匯價一度下行來至 1.1183，終場收在 1.1202，週線下跌 37點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
  預估本週 EUR/USD震盪偏空，主要原因如下：一、上週公佈歐元區10月 PMI
顯示德、法表現分化，法國製造業及服務業均表現疲弱低於50榮枯線，拖累歐元區
10 月綜合 PMI 由 9 月 53.1 下滑至 51.9，為四個月以來新低，經濟基本面承壓將不
利 EUR/USD上行；此外，法國社會及政經局勢不穩導致法債殖利率突破5%，市場
對其財政及政治危機擔憂持續，也將不利歐元表現。二、美國上週初請失業金人數
降至 19.4萬人，為三個月以來新低，顯示勞動力市場依然穩健，且10月 S&P製造
業 PMI為 56.0續創 53個月新高，服務業及綜合PMI維持穩健，顯示美國經濟仍具
韌性，預期將限縮 EUR/USD上行空間。三、技術面而言，在日線圖中，EUR/USD
各天期均線維持空頭排列，且匯價持續承壓於月線之下，顯示短線空方趨勢仍未改
變，在能有效突破上方月線前仍以震盪偏空看待。綜合以上，預估 EUR/USD 本週
呈現震盪偏空的可能性較高。 
＜預估本週＞ 歐元兌美元：1.1120-1.1270

7  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
新台幣利率走勢分析

115.10.19-115.10.22  
銀行隔夜拆款利率 0.805% ~ 0.825%  
30天期短票利率   1.67% ~ 1.68%  
  公債－10年期- A15109 2.000%~2.002% 
      － 5 年期- A15108     1.88%

一、 台幣貨幣市場部分，儘管市場預期強烈颱風「薔琵」來襲，週五金融市場可能
因颱風假而暫停交易，銀行間多預留帳上資金以應不時之需，但市況仍屬充
裕，即便有新增調度需求，也很容易滿足，令短率朝區間低位移動。 
週四隔夜拆款利率區間在 0.805%~0.825%， 30 天期短票主要成交在
1.65%~1.68%，利率低位較上週下跌約 1 個基本點。

二、 本週市場交投冷清，10 年期指標券 A15109 週四於等殖系統成交在 2.002%，
5 年期指標券 A15108則未成交，最近成交利率為 1.88%。 
週二 10 年期公債標售得標利率 2.001%，落於市場預期區間下緣，並創 2008
年以來新高，顯示市場持續反映經濟成長與通膨預期。 
考量通膨前景及國際債市走勢仍具不確定性，市場觀望氣氛濃厚；惟第四季公
債供給相對有限，可望抑制殖利率大幅彈升。預期短線台債殖利率維持區間整
理，5 年券及 10 年券交易區間分別估於 1.82%~1.84%及 1.98%~2.01%。

## Document 058: 2026年10月美联储议息会议点评：加息暂歇，鹰声未止

Source PDF: all report pdf/2026年10月美联储议息会议点评：加息暂歇，鹰声未止.pdf
Report source: 工银国际证券
Date: 2026-11-08
Pages: 4
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

宏观经济深度研究 - 2026 年 10 月 29 日 1 
证券研究 
宏观经济深度研究 
加息暂歇，鹰声未止 
——2026 年 10 月美联储议息会议点评

北京时间 10 月 29 日凌晨，美联储议息会议宣布保持联邦基金利率目标区间不
变在 3.75%—4.00%。近期美国经济仍有韧性，二季度美国实际 GDP 环比折年
增速由此前公布的 1.5%上修至 2.2%，消费和私人投资仍构成主要支撑，其中数
据中心等 AI 相关信息基础设施投资保持较强动能。劳动力市场边际降温，就业
增长和工资压力有所放缓，失业率小幅升至 4.2%，但尚未出现明显恶化。通胀
方面，能源及生产端成本压力有所增强，通胀风险仍未消退。美联储虽具备暂
缓加息、进一步观察政策传导的空间，但在通胀风险尚未充分消退的背景下，
维持限制性政策立场仍有必要。判断当前货币政策是否具有足够的限制性，关
键不仅在于政策利率本身，更在于其相对于自然利率的水平。美联储近期工作
论文指出，持续的财政赤字和政府债务积累可能推升长期自然利率，进而抬高
货币政策的中性基准，使相同水平的政策利率较过去具有更弱的实际限制性。
近年来美国财政赤字和公共债务长期处于高位，这一结构性变化意味着美联储
可能需要维持更高的实际政策利率，才能实现与过去相近的需求抑制效果。展
望未来，政策路径仍取决于经济韧性、通胀黏性与需求降温之间的相对变化。
若经济活动继续保持韧性、失业率维持在当前水平附近，同时核心通胀未能持
续回落，美联储仍存在进一步加息的理由。反之，若就业、消费和资本开支出
现持续走弱，带动通胀回落，美联储继续加息的必要性也将相应下降。 
就业降温打开暂停窗口，通胀仍是主要约束。北京时间 10 月 29 日凌晨，美联
储议息会议宣布保持联邦基金利率目标区间不变在 3.75%—4.00%。近期美国经
济呈现增长韧性延续、就业边际降温与通胀压力回升并存的格局。 经济增长方
面， 2026 年二季度美国实际 GDP 环比折年增速由此前公布的 1.5%上修至
2.2%，一季度增速亦由 2.1%上修至 2.5%。反映私人部门内生需求的实际私人国
内最终销售二季度环比折年增长 4.6%，较一季度明显加快。消费和私人投资仍
构成主要支撑，其中数据中心等 AI 相关信息基础设施投资保持较强动能。劳动
力市场方面，9 月美国新增非农就业仅 2.9 万人，7-8 月合计下修 6 万人，企业
新增用工需求较年中明显降温。失业率小幅升至 4.2%，自 3 月以来始终处于
4.1%—4.3%的窄幅区间。劳动参与率升至 61.8%，就业人口率亦小幅升至
59.2%。工资压力继续缓和， 9 月平均时薪环比仅上涨 0.1%，同比增速降至
3.0%。综合来看，劳动力市场有所降温，但尚未出现明显恶化的迹象。 通胀方
面，9 月 CPI 环比上涨 0.6%，同比升至 3.7%，其中汽油价格上涨 9.1%，贡献总
体 CPI 月度涨幅的一半以上，能源价格整体上涨 5.2%。核心 CPI 环比上涨
0.3%、同比上涨 2.6%，显示基础价格压力仍未完全消退。生产者价格压力更为
突出，9 月 PPI 环比上涨 1.3%、同比上涨 6.3%，最终需求商品价格环比上涨
2.6%；剔除食品、能源和贸易服务后仍环比上涨 0.5%、同比上涨 5.0%。美联储
最新褐皮书显示，多地企业继续报告能源、关税及其他投入成本上升，部分企
业通过提价或燃油附加费向下游转嫁成本。因此，在就业市场尚未明显失速的
情况下，美联储仍有必要维持限制性政策立场。本次保持利率不变并不意味着
通胀风险已经消退。未来是否需要进一步加息，则主要取决于能源及价格压力
是否进一步向核心通胀扩散，以及就业降温是否演变为更广泛的需求走弱。 
自然利率中枢上移可能削弱既定政策利率的实际约束。 判断货币政策是否具有
足够的限制性，关键在于判断实际政策利率相对于自然利率 /长期中性实际利率
的距离。自然利率通常指在通胀稳定、经济运行于潜在水平附近时，使储蓄与
投资实现均衡的实际利率水平。当自然利率上升时，即使政策利率保持不变，
其对总需求所形成的约束也会相应减弱。美联储近期工作论文从自然利率角度

### Page 2

宏观经济深度研究 - 2026 年 10 月 29 日 2 
证券研究 
研究了政府债务扩张的长期影响：论文利用 2021 年佐治亚州参议院决选构造自
然实验：在总统和众议院选举结果已经基本被金融市场消化的情况下，两席参
议院选举主要决定新政府能否通过预算协调程序实施额外财政刺激，因此选举
结果形成了一个相对独立于同期货币政策和经济周期变化的预期债务冲击。研
究估算，该事件使市场预期的额外财政刺激增加约 4500 亿美元、相当于当时
GDP 的约 2.1%，并进一步利用期限结构模型将实际国债收益率变化分解为长期
自然利率和实际期限溢价。结果显示，预期政府债务占 GDP 比重每提高 1 个百
分点，长期实际国债收益率约上升 3—4 个基点，其中长期自然利率上升约 1—2
个基点，实际期限溢价上升约 2—3 个基点。研究进一步利用 1985—2025 年的
中期债务预测进行时间序列检验，发现未来债务率每提高 1 个百分点，长期自
然利率约提高 1.24 个基点，说明政府债务增加对均衡实际利率存在统计上显著
且相对稳定的影响。尽管债务扩张对长期利率的影响更多体现为期限溢价上
升，但其对自然利率的影响仍具有统计显著性。这意味着持续的财政赤字和债
务积累可能逐步抬高货币政策的中性基准，使相同水平的政策利率具有较过去
更弱的实际限制性。 
未来政策路径仍然偏紧。 近年来美国财政赤字和公共债务持续处于较高水平，
使自然利率变化的政策意义进一步上升。美国国会预算办公室预计， 2026 财年
联邦财政赤字约为 1.9 万亿美元、相当于 GDP 的 5.8%，公众持有的联邦债务约
为 GDP 的 101%，并预计在 2036 年进一步升至 120%左右。持续较高的政府融
资需求意味着，财政因素可能逐步成为影响长期均衡实际利率的重要结构性力
量。若长期自然利率较疫情前有所抬升，相同水平的政策利率相对于自然利率
的正向缺口将有所收窄，对总需求形成的抑制作用也会相应减弱。因此，从自
然利率视角看，本次暂停加息并不意味着当前利率水平已经形成充分的限制
性，更多反映 9 月加息后的政策传导仍需观察，就业增长有所放缓也提高了连
续加息的门槛。考虑到持续财政赤字和政府债务积累不仅可能通过短期需求渠
道增加通胀压力，也可能通过改变长期储蓄与资金需求的均衡关系，对中性实
际利率形成上行压力。这意味着，在其他条件相同的情况下，美联储可能需要
维持更高的实际政策利率，才能形成与过去相近的需求抑制效果。展望未来，
如果经济活动继续保持韧性、失业率稳定在当前水平附近，同时核心通胀未能
持续回落，则美联储仍有进一步提高政策利率、扩大实际政策利率相对于自然
利率正向缺口的理由。参考 9 月 SEP，2026 年和 2027 年末联邦基金利率预测中
位数为 4.1%，这一路径仍与再加息 25 个基点大体一致。相反，如果未来就业、
消费和资本开支出现持续走弱，通胀同时回落，则实际政策利率还会在名义利
率不变的情况下被动上升，从而进一步增强货币政策的限制性，美联储继续加
息的必要性也将相应下降。

参考文献： 
Bhatt, A., Diercks, A. M., Eyal, B., & Skaperdas, A. (2026). The Causal Effect of Debt 
on Interest Rates . Finance and Economics Discussion Series 2026-031, Board of 
Governors of the Federal Reserve System.

## Document 084: 2027年春闘の初動モメンタム・アップデート ～経労委報告原案と産別要求からも賃上げ意欲を確認～

Source PDF: all report pdf/2027年春闘の初動モメンタム・アップデート ～経労委報告原案と産別要求からも賃上げ意欲を確認～.pdf
Report source: DLRI
Date: 2026-11-02
Pages: 3
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Economic Trends

2026 年 11 月 2 日

1/3 
2027 年春闘の初動モメンタム・アップデート 
～経労委報告原案と産別要求からも賃上げ意欲を確認～ 
 経済調査部 シニアエグゼクティブエコノミスト  新家 義貴 
（℡：050-5474-7490）

（要旨） 
○ 経団連による、2027 年春闘に向けての経労委報告原案が判明した。高い賃上げの「社会全体
への浸透」を掲げており、大企業を中心に高い賃上げが定着しつつあるなか、その動きを中
小企業などにも広げることに重点を置く姿勢がうかがえる。また、筒井経団連会長の発言か
らは、 ベア実施の検討をスタンダードとする従来の方針が維持されていることも確認できる。 
○ 流通やサービス、繊維などの労働組合で構成されるＵＡゼンセンも定昇込み６％、ベア４％
など、前年並みの高い賃上げ要求を掲げた。労使とも、賃上げに向けての姿勢にトーンダウ
ンはみられない。 
○ 一方、賃上げ率のさらなる加速を示唆する材料は現時点で多くない。連合の要求目安は前年
より強まったものの、経団連は賃上げ率の加速までは示していないとみられ、ＵＡゼンセン
の要求率は前年と同水準である。2027 年春闘では、 ５％近傍という高い賃上げ率を維持でき
るかが焦点となる。 
○ 日銀の植田総裁は、2027 年春闘で２％のインフレ率を前提とした賃金設定が行われることの
重要性を指摘した。2026 年の物価上昇率が前年から鈍化するなかでも高い賃上げが続くかど
うかは、２％の物価安定目標の定着や今後の金融政策を判断するうえで重要となる。

経労委報告原案は、高い賃上げの「社会全体への浸透」を掲げる 
10 月22 日のレポート（2027 年春闘、初動のモメンタムは現時点で良好 ～労働側、経営側とも
昨年からのトーンダウンはみられず～ | 新家 義貴 | 第一ライフ資産運用経済研究所）では、連
合の基本構想や経団連の経労委報告骨子案などをもとに、2027 年春闘に向けた賃上げ姿勢は労使
双方とも前年からのトーンダウンはみられないと評価したが、その後、経団連の経労委報告原案
やＵＡゼンセンの要求方針素案が明らかになった。本稿では、これらの新たな材料を踏まえ、春
闘の初動のモメンタムをアップデートする。 
10 月30 日、経団連が2027 年春闘に向けて取りまとめる「経営労働政策特別委員会報告（経労
委報告） 」の原案内容が報道された。報道によれば、原案では賃上げの力強いモメンタムを継続

### Page 2

2/3 
し、その動きを社会全体に浸透させるために取り組みを強化する方針が示された。特に、中小企
業が継続的・計画的な賃上げを実施できるかが重要とされ、適正な価格転嫁に加え、大企業が持
つ人材や技術を活用して中小企業の収益力向上を支援する必要性が指摘された。また、ＡＩの普
及を踏まえ、働き手が生み出した成果をどのように評価し、賃金などの処遇へ反映するかについ
て、見直しを求めている。生産性向上によって生み出された付加価値を賃金にどう配分するか
は、今後の労使交渉においても重要な課題となるだろう。 
経団連はこれまで、2023 年を賃上げの「起点」 、2024 年を「加速」 、2025 年を「定着」 、2026 年
を「さらなる定着」の年と位置付けてきた。今回の原案では「社会全体への浸透」が掲げられて
おり、大企業を中心に高い賃上げが定着しつつあるなか、その動きを中小企業などにも広げるこ
とに重点を置く姿勢がうかがえる。2026 年春闘向けの経労委報告でも、中小企業における構造的
な賃上げや適正な価格転嫁の重要性が強調されていたが、今回の原案では、大企業による人材・
技術面での支援にも言及するなど、賃上げの裾野を広げるための取り組みをより具体的に示して
いる。 
賃上げに対する経営側の基本姿勢も維持されている。2026 年春闘向けの経労委報告では、ベア
実施の検討を賃金交渉のスタンダードと位置付けていたが、筒井経団連会長は今年９月７日の記
者会見でも、 「ベースアップ実施の検討を引き続きスタンダードと位置付けていきたい」と述べて
いる。ベアに対する従来の方針が維持されていることは重要だ。今春以降のイラン情勢悪化を受
け、原材料費や物流費の上昇によって企業の賃上げ余力が圧迫されることが懸念されていたが、
少なくとも現時点で経団連が賃上げに慎重な姿勢へ転じた様子はうかがえない。

UAゼンセンも前年並みの高い要求を維持 
労働側でも、主要産業別労働組合組織の要求方針が明らかになり始めた。10 月 27 日、流通やサ
ービス、繊維などの労働組合で構成されるＵＡゼンセンは、2027 年春闘の要求方針素案を公表し
た。正社員組合員については、定昇込みで６％、ベア等の賃上げ分で４％を基本とし、賃金水準
が最低基準に満たない組合などでは格差是正分を加えて７％の賃上げを要求する。また、短時間
組合員については、制度昇給分に加えて５％、制度昇給分が明確でない場合には７％を目安とす
るとのことである。 
これらの要求水準は、いずれも2026 年春闘から変わっていない。６～７％という要求水準は依
然として高いが、要求率を前年からさらに引き上げたわけではないことには注意が必要だ。連合
は 2027 年春闘に向けた基本構想で「６％中心、５％以上」と前年より強い要求目安を示したが、
実際には前年の段階で、すでに６％程度の賃上げを要求していた産別組織もある。今回の連合の
方針には、こうした既存の要求水準を追認する側面もあり、ＵＡゼンセンの要求方針はその一例
といえる。

前年からのトーンダウンはみられていないが、加速しているわけでもない  
以上を踏まえると、2027 年春闘の初動のモメンタムは引き続き良好と評価できる。経団連はベ

### Page 3

本資料は情報提供を目的として作成されたものであり、投資勧誘を目的としたものではありません。作成時点で、第一ライフ資産運用
経済研究所が信ずるに足ると判断した情報に基づき作成していますが、その正確性、完全性に対する責任は負いません。見通しは予告
なく変更されることがあります。また、記載された内容は、第一生命保険ないしはその関連会社の投資方針と常に整合的であるとは限
りません。 
3/3 
ア実施の検討をスタンダードとする従来の方針を維持し、高い賃上げを中小企業などにも広げる
ことで「社会全体への浸透」を目指す姿勢を示している。労働側でも、連合が前年より強い要求
目安を掲げており、ＵＡゼンセンも前年並みの高い要求水準を維持した。イラン情勢悪化に伴う
コスト増加が懸念されるなかでも、労使双方の賃上げ姿勢に前年からのトーンダウンはみられな
い。 
もっとも、賃上げ姿勢が慎重化していないことと、賃上げ率がさらに高まることは別問題であ
る。経団連が重視しているのは高い賃上げの継続とその裾野の拡大であり、賃上げ率の加速を示
唆しているわけではないだろう。また、ＵＡゼンセンの賃上げ要求は高いが、要求率自体は前年
から据え置かれている。連合の要求目安は強まったものの、現時点で賃上げ率の加速を見込むだ
けの材料は揃っていない。エコノミストの予想を集計するＥＳＰフォーキャスト調査（10 月調
査）でも、2027 年春闘の賃上げ率予想は4.96％とされており、前年実績の5.18％から小幅に鈍化
するとの見方となっている（数値は厚生労働省ベース）。 
当面の焦点は、５％近傍という高い賃上げ率を維持できるかどうかにある。もっとも、今後の
経済・物価情勢によっては企業の賃上げ余力や労働側の要求姿勢も変化し得るため、先行きを慎
重に見極める必要がある。当面、自動車総連や電機連合など主要産業別労働組合組織の要求方針
が前年からどの程度変化するかが注目される。 
金融政策の観点からも2027 年春闘の動向は重要である。日銀の植田総裁は10 月 30 日の記者会
見で、基調的な物価上昇率が２％の物価安定の目標と概ね整合的な水準に達したとの認識を示し
た上で、2027 年春闘について、労使交渉でも２％のインフレ率が前提として織り込まれることが
必要との考えを示した。一方、2026 年の物価上昇率が過去２～３年より低くなる可能性を指摘
し、それが春闘の結果にどう影響するかを注視する姿勢も示している。 
ここ数年の春闘では、物価上昇に負けない賃上げを実現することが、高い賃上げ要求を支える
重要な要因となってきた。2026 年の物価上昇率は 2025 年と比べれば鈍化する見込みであり、こう
した観点からの賃上げ圧力はやや弱まる可能性がある。 
もっとも、労使双方が今後も２％程度の物価上昇が続くことを前提として賃金を設定するよう
になれば、足元の物価上昇率が低下しても安定的な賃上げは持続しやすくなる。日銀は基調的な
物価上昇率が２％に「到達」したと判断したが、２％の物価安定目標が「定着」したとまでは判
断していない。その意味でも、2027 年春闘は、２％の物価上昇を前提とした賃金設定がどの程度
定着しているかを確認する機会となる。春闘に向けた労使双方の姿勢や実際の賃上げ動向は、今
後の日銀の金融政策判断においても重要な材料となるだろう。

Lite-On Tech

Upgrade to Buy; 3Q26 beat, better margin; 
solid AI outlook; lift PO to NT$288

Rating Change: BUY | PO: 288.00 TWD | Price: 240.50 TWD

Upgrade to Buy and raise PO to NT$288  
We upgrade Lite-On Tech (LoT) to Buy from Neutral with a higher PO of NT$288 (18x 
2027E EPS; prior NT$285 with valuation unchanged). We turn more positive on LoT 
after two straight quarters of results beat, especially with margin surprise. We lift 2026-
28E EPS by 1-8% (Exhibit 1), with still solid AI power-supply demand/order. Our 2026-
28E EPS is 4-7% above consensus (Exhibit 2). We expect the market to react more 
positively to LoT, with 3Q26 results beat, vs. Delta with 3Q26 results miss (see Robert 
Cheng’s Delta 3Q26 note), amid similar positive comments on long-term AI market 
demand. 
3Q26 beat: better margins, despite lighter sales 
LoT’s 3Q26 GM/OPM of 25.9%/15.1% came in better than BofAe of 25.3%/13.5% and 
consensus of 25.1%/13.6%, despite lighter sales (3%/5% below BofAe/consensus). We 
view the QoQ GM/OPM decline from record high in 2Q26 as largely expected as mgmt. 
had indicated at 2Q26 meeting that 1H26 GM of 24.7% represented a more sustainable 
level. Besides AI, better margins were also helped by improving product mix in IT/CE (i.e. 
higher mix from high-end ICT and LEO satellite) and higher automation level.  
4Q26 sales up QoQ; 2H26 GM above 1H; rising AI mix 
Similar to Delta, LoT also sees short-term shipment uncertainties/push-outs due to 
component shortage and power/electricity shortage for AI datacenter infrastructure. 
Nevertheless, the CEO remains long-term positive on AI demand. He also expects easing 
component/power constraints from 4Q26. Yet, mgmt. expects 4Q26 cloud sales to grow 
QoQ/YoY, led by ASIC/GPU/AMD projects, and 2H26 GM to be above 1H26 level (BofAe: 
26.3% in 4Q26, vs. 25.9%/24.7% in 3Q26/1H26). The CEO indicated 800VDC power rack 
mass shipment in 1Q27, after sample shipment in 4Q26. Mgmt. expects AI sales mix at 
30%+ in 2026 and 40-50% in 2027. This time, LoT raised 2026 capex to NT$19.7bn 
(prior NT$18.0bn) and sees higher level in 2027 (BofAe: NT$20.0bn).

Company Sector 
Electronics Manufacturing Services

Company Description 
Lite-On Tech is a leading PC and server power-supply maker 
globally. Besides PC/server power-supply, its key products 
include optoelectronics (photo-coupler, LED lighting, 
outdoor lighting, auto lighting) and power-supply for game 
console and industrial. Key applications include AIoT, 5G, 
and smart life. Its key customers include HP, Dell, Lenovo, 
Microsoft, Sony, Nintendo, and global cloud hyperscalers. 
Lite-On Tech was founded in 1975 and headquartered in 
Taiwan.

Investment Rationale 
We have a Buy rating on Lite-On Tech in view of (1) its solid 
position in AI server power-supply as the second largest 
component maker globally and (2) expanding margins with 
improving product mix, rising scale, and better operating 
efficiency. We see consistently high cash dividend payout 
(over 50% since 2008) and solid balance sheet as share 
price downside supports.

Upgrade to Buy; raise PO to NT$288  
We upgrade Lite-On Tech (LoT) to Buy, from Neutral, with a higher PO of NT$288 (18x 
2027E EPS) from NT$285 with valuation unchanged. We turn more positive on LoT after 
two straight quarters of results beat, especially with margin surprise. We lift 2026-28E 
EPS by 1-8% (Exhibit 1) to reflect 3Q26 results beat, the latest guidance, and solid AI 
power-supply demand/order. Our 2026-28E EPS is 4-7% above consensus (Exhibit 2).  
LoT’s share price corrected 21% and 14% in the recent three months and one month, 
respectively, vs. key peer Delta’s (2308 TT; covered by Robert Cheng) -27%/-29% and 
TAIEX’s -2%/-6%. We expect more positive market reaction to LoT’s 3Q26 results beat, 
vs. Delta’s 3Q26 results miss (see Robert Cheng’s Delta 3Q26 note), amid similar 
positive comments on long-term AI market demand. LoT also has a cheaper valuation vs. 
Delta. LoT currently trades at 15x 2027E P/E, below Delta’s 24x and overall peers’ 
average of 20x (Exhibit 6).

3Q26 beat: better margins, despite lighter sales 
LoT posted 3Q26 results and held the physical analyst meeting on 30 Oct after market 
close. 3Q26 GM/OPM of 25.9%/15.1% came in better than BofAe of 25.3%/13.5% and 
consensus of 25.1%/13.6%, despite lighter sales (3%/5% below BofAe/consensus; +9% 
QoQ, +28% YoY). We view the QoQ decline in GM/OPM from the record highs in 2Q26

### Page 4

as largely expected, as management had already indicated at the 2Q26 meeting that the 
1H26 GM of 24.7% represented a more sustainable level (see our 2Q26 result note). 
Besides AI, we believe better margins were also helped by improving product mix in 
IT/CE (i.e. higher mix from high-end ICT and LEO satellite) and higher automation level. 
With non-operating gain of NT$1.1bn (including net interest income of NT$369mn and 
FX gain of NT$734mn) and higher minority interest, net income (+4% QoQ, +60% YoY) 
was 12%/11% above BofAe/consensus.

BofA GLOBAL RESEARCH

Similar comments on material/power shortage like Delta 
Similar to Delta mgmt.’s comments at 3Q26 analyst meeting, LoT also sees short-term 
shipment uncertainties/push-outs due to material/component shortage (e.g. semis, PCB, 
passives) and power/electricity shortage for AI datacenter infrastructure. Nevertheless, 
LoT’s CEO remains long-term positive on AI demand with ongoing platform/spec 
upgrade. He also expects easing component/power constraints from 4Q26, with ongoing 
strategic component procurement/preparation and customers’ robust AI demand.

4Q26 sales up QoQ; 2H26 GM above 1H; rising AI mix 
Into 4Q26, mgmt. expects cloud sales to grow both QoQ and YoY. Key drivers include (1) 
volume production of 8.5kW PSUs for ASIC applications and BBUs, next-generation 
Nvidia Vera Rubin 110kW solutions, 42-inch power shelves for AMD Helios, (2) prototype 
shipments of 800VDC power rack, and (3) continued momentum in high-end power 
applications, with shipments of power solutions for LEO satellites. the CEO also expects 
2H26 GM to be above 1H26 level. We thus project 4Q26 overall sales to grow 5% QoQ 
(+36% YoY), with GM at 26.3% (vs. 25.9%/24.7% in 3Q26/1H26). For HVDC, the CEO 
further stated 800VDC power rack mass shipment in 1Q27, after final validation and 
sample shipment in 4Q26. Mgmt. kept AI sales mix target at 30%+ in 2026 and 
potentially 40-50% in 2027. LoT raised 2026 capex to NT$19.7bn (vs. NT$18.0bn guided 
at 2Q26 analyst meeting) and expects a higher level in 2027 (BofAe: NT$20.0bn).

Initially expects 2027 sales/GM/OPM YoY increase 
Into 2027, the CEO initially expects cloud sales to grow another 70% YoY, driven by 
rising ASP and power-supply product/spec upgrade. He targets another 10% YoY sales 
growth for optoelectronics business, driven by industrial and new energy business. He 
expects LEO satellite power to grow YoY but sees flattish sales from conventional ICT 
power business. Nevertheless, he expects GM/OPM YoY expansion in 2027. We thus 
expect 2027 sales to grow 38% YoY, with GM/OPM increasing by 1.6ppt/1.5ppt YoY.

### Page 7

Price objective basis & risk 
Delta Electronics Inc. (DLTEF) 
Our PO of NT$2,250 is based on 30x 4Q27-3Q28E EPS, which is close to 2.0 standard 
deviations above Delta's average trading P/E since 2012. We believe the P/E multiple is 
justified, given (1) continuous strength and leading position in server power-supply and 
power choke business, (2) content/value growth with broader product offering in AI 
(artificial intelligence) business, (3) rising cooling sales by targeting top CSPs' 
customized projects, and (4) ongoing margin expansion.

The upside risks to our PO are (1) stronger-than-expected server/datacenter demand, (2) 
faster-than-expected IA recovery, and (3) faster-than-expected EV model launches by 
global brands.

The downside risks to our PO are (1) unexpected changes in policy that could impact IA 
growth in China, (2) slower-than-expected server/datacenter demand recovery, (3) 
earnings impact from unprofitable long-term investments (4) Adverse effects of 
announced USITC 337 investigation into multiple companies in the AI supply chain, 
which includes Delta Electric. 
Lite-On Tech (LOTZF) 
Our PO of NT$288 is based on 18x 2027E EPS, which is around 1.75SD above its 
historical average since 2013. We believe the P/E multiple is justified, given its solid 
market position to capture multi-year AI server power-supply market demand with 
improving product mix. Yet, we also assign a valuation discount to its key peer, given its 
late entry and a slower progress in cooling business, smaller scale, and lighter margin 
profile.

Downside risks: (1) weaker-than-expected demand from server/cloud power-supply 
business, (2) lighter margin profile for automotive LED lighting with limited ASP 
expansion, (3) pricing competition and component price increase from power-supply 
business, and (4) less order win from cooling business.

Upside risks: (1) stronger-than-expected margin profile for automotive LED lighting with 
ASP expansion, (2) faster revenue growth from server/cloud power-supply with a more 
positive datacenter market outlook, and (3) larger/stronger order win from cooling 
business.

Idea
3Q preview: Co-Tech is scheduled to report its 3Q results on November 6. We
estimate 3Q revenue at NT$3,169mn (+13% q/q, +60% y/y), primarily driven by
higher copper prices and product-mix shifts. We lower our 3Q GM estimate from
28.2% to 27.0% (-30bps q/q, +650bps y/y), reflecting the impact of higher
production costs and copper prices during the quarter. Co-Tech has already reported
preliminary monthly profits for August and September at the request of the TWSE

(report link); combined August and September net profit now accounts for
approximately 67% of our 3Q estimate. We estimate 3Q net profit at NT$610mn
(+12% q/q, +141% y/y), implying EPS of NT$2.41.
4Q GM should improve sequentially: We expect 4Q revenue to decline slightly q/q
to NT$3,074mn (-3% q/q, +42% y/y), primarily due to the end of production for low-
end HTE copper foil and the lack of new capacity coming online. However, we expect
Co-Tech’s GM to expand 700bps q/q to 34%, driven by an improved product mix, a

~10% processing-fee increase effective from 4Q onward, and lower electricity rates
compared with 2Q–3Q, when summer electricity rates were in effect. We forecast
4Q EPS of NT$3.02 (+25% q/q, +102% y/y). For CY26, we estimate revenue will
increase approximately 47% y/y to NT$11.6bn, with GM expanding 760bps y/y to
29.3%, implying EPS of NT$9.62 (+129% y/y).
Potential processing-fee hike: Our supply-chain checks suggest that a key
competitor may announce processing-fee increases as early as December. If so, we

expect Co-Tech to follow with a price increase as well.
Upward revisions to our capacity assumptions ( Exhibit 3 ): Co-Tech has
announced plans to expand its greenfield capacity in Malaysia (report link). The
Malaysian expansion will be implemented in two phases, adding approximately 20k
tons of annual HVLP3/4 capacity from late 2028 onward. RG-series capacity is also
expected to increase modestly through the conversion of older HTE production
lines.

Raised PT to NT$745; stay OW: The increase in our price target is driven by our
earnings estimate changes for CY26-28. Our new price target implies 21x CY28 P/E
and the stock currently trades at only 12x CY28 P/E. We also find the valuation
attractive compared to  its past 3Y P/E average of ~20x.

Price target and valuation methodology
Our price target for Co-Tech increases  ~2% to NT$745: This implies 39x CY27 or 21x
CY28 P/E. Our price target is also our base case scenario value. It is derived from our

residual income (RI) model (similar to the rest of our tech hardware universe).
To calculate the fair value of the stock, we use the company's beginning equity plus the
present value of all expected residual income of earnings in excess of cost of capital. The
RI is positive when ROAE is above the cost of capital and negative when it is below the
cost of capital. Our key assumptions include:
•  A cost of equity of 9.3% (beta of 1.0, equity risk premium of 8.7%, and risk-free rate

of 1.0%),
• a medium-term growth rate of 11.5% (lowered from 13% previously, as we enter a
higher base in CY27),
• a terminal growth rate of 3%.
The 21x CY28 P/E implied by our price target is above its  past 3Y average  of 20x. We deem
this valuation as fair, because Co-Tech is benefitting from an earnings growth cycle fueled
by stronger AI server/networking CCL demand, Co-Tech's continued mix improvement
(from HTE + RTF (RG) copper foils to advanced RTF (RG) + HVLP3/4 copper foils) and

continued  processing fee adjustments, which supports margin expansion. The stock is
trading at just 12x CY28 P/E, below Co-Tech's past 3Y average of 20x. Thus, we view the
risk reward as attractive.
Our bull- and bear-case values also increase ~2% to NT$1,225 and NT$305,
respectively.

STOCK RATINGS
Morgan Stanley uses a relative rating system using terms such as Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated or Underweight (see definitions below). Morgan Stanley does not assign ratings of Buy,
Hold or Sell to the stocks we cover. Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated and Underweight are not the equivalent of buy, hold and sell.  Investors should carefully read the definitions of all
ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision
to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

### Page 1

3Q26 EPS of NT$6.58 topped our/BBG estimates by 2%/7% — 3Q26 sales 
reached NT$10.6bn +10% y/y, broadly in-line with our/BBG estimates, helped 
mostly by stronger garment sales +16% y/y thanks to persisting strong strength for 
new products/vertical orders from emerging brands. GPM expanded 1.2ppt q/q to 
31.2%, beating our/BBG forecast by 30bps and 70bps, which is attributable to price 
hikes in 2Q to reflect cost inflation, alongside a richer mix of vertical orders, and a 
lower contribution from carry-over styles. The opex ratio declined to 9.9% from 
10.8% in 2Q, helping OPM to reach 21.3%, which beat BBG's 20.1%. 
Oct-26 sales boosted by sustained vertical order strength  — Oct-26 sales rose 
11% y/y to NT$3.4bn, driven by garment +16% y/y while fabrics sales were broadly 
flat y/y. Growth continued to benefit from high-margin vertical orders from 
emerging brands. Mgmt expects 4Q26E sales to rise sequentially, supported by 
sustained demand for new products, while positive FX, product mix upgrade and 
price increases should help maintain GPM from 3Q's level. Order visibility extends 
into early 2Q27E, with mgmt remaining optimistic about 1Q27E orders.  
Implications — The margin recovery supports our view that earlier cost pressures 
are easing, while Eclat's product differentiation and growing exposure to emerging 
brands continue to drive higher value vertical orders. Order visibility extending into 
early 2Q27E and an optimistic 1Q27E outlook provide further support for earnings

Valuation
Our target price for Eclat of NT$460 is based on 17x 2H26H-1H27E EPS, 15% below its historical average in view of persisting
uncertainties on consumption trend and oil price fluctuations.
Risks
Key downside risks that could prevent the shares from reaching our target price include: rising tariff tensions, conservative
consumer spending, slower capacity ramp-up in Indonesia, softer order outlooks by brand customers, and a lukewarm macro

environment.

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.

Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment

rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient
valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating

and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of
information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price

chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and
investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the

expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.
Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.
Catalyst Watch/Short Term Views (“STV”) Ratings Disclosure:

Catalyst Watch and STV Upside/Downside calls: Citi Research may also include a Catalyst Watch or STV Upside or Downside
call to indicate the analyst expects the share price to rise (fall) in absolute terms over a specified period of 30 or 90 days in
reaction to one or more specific near-term catalysts or events impacting the company or the market. A Catalyst Watch will be
published when Analyst confidence is high that an impact to share price will occur; it will be a STV when confidence level is

moderate. A Catalyst Watch or STV Upside/Downside call will automatically expire at the end of the specified 30/90 day
period. The Catalyst Watch will also be automatically removed if share price performance (calculated at market close) exceeds
15% against the direction of the call (unless over-ridden by the analyst). The analyst may also remove a Catalyst Watch or STV
call prior to the end of the specified period in a published research note. A Catalyst Watch/STV Upside or Downside call may be

聯亞(3081.TT) 
產業：電子–通信網路 
矽光需求持續強勁，更多設備投產與新增後段外包夥伴帶動產能倍增  
 投資評價與建議 
受惠CW Laser出貨量持續增加、新購顯影設備逐步進駐以及後段代工廠產能
開出，預估稅後EPS為21.01/49.3/97.15元，考量NPO和CPO技術逐步成熟且
聯亞具備多波⻑雷射領先技術，從2028年獲利評價，給予40倍本益比。 
 重點評論分析 
矽光產品出貨增加 ， 3Q26營收成⻑ ， 主要客⼾已下⻑期訂單 ， 4Q26營收攀升 ：
3Q26營收季增27.9%至15.6億元 ， 係因矽光產品出貨增加 ， 毛利率因營收規模
擴大上升1.11ppts至58.6%，營業費用較2Q26增加約1,400萬元至1.43億元，
主要來自研發費用以及隨獲利增加按比例提列之員工分紅費用，稅後EPS為
6.12元。4Q26矽光產品需求持續強勁，主要客戶皆已開始下6個⽉以上的⻑期
訂單，訂單能見度佳，隨公司本身與下游代工廠產能逐步開出，預期矽光產品
出貨量逐季成⻑，預估4Q26營收季增23.5%至19.3億元，稅後EPS為7.19元。  
設備與後段代工產能開出， 2027和2028年總產量連續兩年皆年增 150%：展
望2027~2028年，1.6T矽光產品已量產，3.2T與NPO/CPO應用之更高功率矽
光產品則和客戶持續合作開發和驗證中 ， 針對特定客戶之資料中心200G PD已
完成驗證且磊晶產能已規劃，預計未來數⽉將開始量產。目前E-Beam、奈米
壓印技術與DUV深紫外光微影三種技術皆同步進行擴產，隨更多E-Beam投
產，1Q27產能將明顯增加，奈米壓印設備預計於2026年底進廠。 
 投資風險 
設備與後段產能開出進度不如預期。

### Page 2

2

| 營運概況  
矽光產品出貨持續增加與後段產能開出，3Q26營收成⻑，主要客⼾已下⻑期訂單，4Q26營收持續攀升： 3Q26營收季
增27.9%至15.6億元 ， 係因矽光產品出貨量增加 ， 毛利率因營收規模擴大上升1.11ppts至58.6% ， 營業利益為7.71億元，
營業利益率為49.4%，營業費用較2Q26增加約1,400萬元至1.43億元，主要來自研發費用以及隨獲利增加按比例提列之
員工分紅費用，折舊費用較2Q26增加160萬元至7,660萬元，稅後淨利為6.21億元，稅後EPS為6.12元。4Q26矽光產品
需求持續強勁，主要客戶皆已開始下6個⽉以上的⻑期訂單，訂單能見度佳，隨公司本身與下游代工廠產能逐步開出，
預期矽光產品出貨量逐季成⻑，預估4Q26營收季增23.5%至19.3億元，稅後EPS為7.19元。影響毛利率因素有三：整體
營收與產出、匯率變化以及成本。管理層預期營收於接下來幾季將逐季成⻑，惟3Q26前半段台幣升值，隨後9⽉底台幣
轉貶值，匯率變化對3Q26毛利率產生些許不利影響，加上更多人員召募以及設備折舊成本逐步反映，且產品訂價方面，
聯亞僅維持賺取合理利潤，將無法持續大幅漲價，導致毛利率於接下來幾季成⻑幅度將趨緩。 
設備與後段代工產能開出，2027 和 2028 年總產量連續兩年皆年增 150%：展望 2027~2028 年，聯亞 1.6T 矽光產品
已經開始量產，3.2T 與 NPO/CPO 應用之更高功率矽光產品則和客戶持續合作開發和驗證中，另外過去受限產能，公
司設備主要用於生產 LD，自 2027 年開始，針對特定客戶之資料中心 200G 以上規格之 PD 已完成驗證，並已規劃相對
應磊晶產能，將於未來數⽉將開始量產，惟出貨量仍遠小於 LD。2026年 8 ⽉董事會決議與 1 美系資料中心客戶簽訂為
期 4 年之⻑期供貨合約，為滿足該客戶之需求，公司追加約 30 億元資本支出，該資本支出購置之設備可帶動 2027 和
2028 年總產量連續兩年皆年增 150%，另外因應磷化銦基板缺料問題，聯亞目前與 2 家日本主要供應商與 1 家美國主
要供應商達成未來數年的⻑期供貨合約，且美系客戶亦提供部分資金，協助確保後續大規模量產之基板供應無虞。因應
產能不足，聯亞持續新增後段外包合作夥伴位，台灣地區增加數家大型集團作為合作夥伴，該集團在光通訊領域投入意
願高，其資本支出承諾與實力遠高於早期合作夥伴，有助於與聯亞共同爭取國際 AI 訂單。曝光顯影製程方面，目前 E-
Beam、奈米壓印技術與 DUV 深紫外光微影三種技術皆同步進行擴產，首部新購置之 E-Beam 設備已於 2026 年 7 ⽉
開始量產，帶動 7 ⽉營收大幅躍升，隨更多 E-Beam 與 MOCVD 設備投產，1Q27 產能將有明顯增加且未來 8 個季度
產能將逐步增加 ， 奈米壓印設備預計於 2026 年底進場 ， DUV 深紫外光微影設備則於 2027 年以後加入生產 。 綜上所述 ，
預估 2026/2027/2028 年營收年增 155%/130%/86%至56.2/129.1/239.5 億元，稅後 EPS 為 21.01/49.3/97.15 元。

買進：未來六個⽉內潛在漲幅 30%以上。 
增加持股：未來六個⽉內潛在漲幅 15~30%(含)。

降低持股：未來六個⽉內潛在跌幅 15%以上。

## Document 229: [005380] 현대차(005380) 저점을 받쳐줄 실적현대차(005380) 저점을 받쳐줄 실적저점을 받쳐줄 실적 - 기업

Source PDF: all report pdf/[005380] 현대차(005380) 저점을 받쳐줄 실적현대차(005380) 저점을 받쳐줄 실적저점을 받쳐줄 실적 - 기업.pdf

목표주가 500,000

현재주가 
(26.10.29) 
292,500 
자동차업종

◼ 3Q26 비용절감/환율 효과로 우려 대비 양호한 실적 시현 
◼ 2027년 물량–환율 영향 상쇄되며 2H27 이익체력 개선 본격화 예상 
◼ 1) Physical AI(로봇/SDV) 경쟁력, 2) 주요국 점유율 반등에 주목

투자의견 매수 유지, 목표주가 50만원 하향(-6%) 
현대차 TP 50만원(시총 102조원)은 12MF EPS 41,978원에 Target PER 12배
적용하여 산출. 제한적인 실적 기대 반영해 타겟 밸류 하향함에 따라 TP 조정  
우려 대비 양호한 3Q26 실적으로 주가 하방 부담 완화. 주가의 추세적 상승 
위해서는 1) Physical AI(로봇/SDV) 경쟁력, 2) 주요국 점유율 반등 확인돼야 할 
것. 기저효과에 따른 물량 개선이 주가 저점 받쳐줄 것이나, 현재 밸류(2027년 
PER 9.1배vs 글로벌 피어 6.1배)에서 추세 상승 이끌긴 어려울 것이기 때문. 
이에, 자동차 본업 외 1) 한/미국 Physical AI 투자 계획 및 2) BD 공급망 구축 
/양산 가시화에 따른 주가 반등 기대

3Q26 매출 45조(YoY -3%, 이하 YoY), OP 2.7조(+6%), OPM 5.9% 시현 
3Q26 OP 추정(2.5조)/컨센(2.4조) 상회. 파업에 따른 생산/판매 차질 영향으로 
기대 제한적이었으나, 비용 절감/환율 영향으로 우려 대비 양호한 실적 시현. 
[자동차] 매출 33조(-10%), OP 2.1조(+25%), OPM 6.5%. 도매 판매 92.1만대
(-11%). 판매 부진과 원자재 부담에도 불구, 환효과(+1.3조)/비용절감(+0.5조)
로 실적 방어. 이외 YoY 변수별 영향은 물량(-1.1조)/믹스(-0.6조)/원자재(-
0.5조). [금융] 매출 9.2조(+28%), OP 4,710억(-18%), OPM 5.1%, [기타] 매출 
2.9조(+2%), OP 712억(-72%), OPM 2.5% 기록

2027년 매출 191조(YoY +2%, 이하 YoY), OP 12.7조(+15%), OPM 6.6% 전망 
기저효과 바탕으로 연결 도매 물량 YoY +5% 증가 예상하나, 원달러 환율 -
4% 하락(가정 환율 1,380원)하며 1H27 → 2H27로가며 이익체력 개선 예상 
(단위: 십억원, %)

### Page 5

DAISHIN SECURITIES

5  
  현대차 3Q26 실적 컨퍼런스콜 QnA 
Q. 금융부문 환율 변동에 따른 4Q26 매출 및 손익 영향 
- 금융부문 환율 영향은 현대차 실적에 미치는 환율 영향과 동일한 방향으로 발생

Q. 3Q26 금융부문 수익성 악화 요인 
- 장기금리 상승에 따른 조달 및 이자비용 증가가 주요인. 자산/부채 만기 매칭에 따른 
금리 상승 영향 반영 
- EV 중심 중고차 가격지수 소폭 하락 및 차량 반환에 따른 매각손실, 금리 상승에 따
른 연체율 및 신용비용 상승 
- 차량 판매가격 인하 영향은 없으며, 3Q26 도매 판매 감소에 따른 금융 영업수익 영향 
불가피했음

Q. 향후 주주환원 정책에서 배당 및 자사주 매입 우선순위 
- 기존 밸류업 정책: 최소 DPS 1만원/TPR 35% 제시. 연간 배당 약 2.6조원 → TPR 
35% 충족 위한 잔여분 자사주 매입/소각 활용 
- 경영환경 변화 고려하여 기존 밸류업 프로그램 전반의 주주가치 제고 방안 재검토중 
- 새로운 정책 확정 및 주주 소통 전까지 기존 약속인 최소 DPS 1만원 유지할 계획 
- 시장의 배당 및 자사주 매입 간 균형 요구를 인지하고 있으며, 주주 의견 수렴 후 주
주가치 제고 계획 공유 예정

Q. 3Q26 영업이익 증감 요인 중 기타 +6,400억 발생 원인 
- 월별 컨틴전시 활동을 통한 비용 최적화 및 재료비 절감 지속 추진 
- 4Q26 원가 절감 효과는 3Q26 대비 확대 예상

Q. 3Q26 영업외손익 감소 원인 
- 3Q26 환율 급락으로 외화 매출채권 등 외화자산 관련 외화환산손실 및 외환거래손실 
발생 
- 매출채권 인식 시점과 회수 시점 간 환율 차이에 따른 외환차손 발생 
- 지분법 대상 기업들의 3Q26 실적 부진에 따른 지분법손익 감소도 일부 영향. 대부분 
외환 관련 손실에서 발생

Q. 중국 OEM 국내 진출 확대에 따른 대응 및 신차/가격 전략 
- 중국 OEM 및 테슬라의 국내 M/S 확대 대응중. 다만, 잦은 가격 조정은 중고차 잔존
가치 및 고객가치 훼손할 수 있어 지양 
- 현대캐피탈의 캡티브 금융 경쟁력 활용. 잔존가치 유예할부 등을 통해 월 납입금을 낮
춰 중국 OEM 대비 경쟁력 확보 
- 서비스 네트워크 기반으로 경쟁사 대비 경쟁력 확보 노력 
- 국내 신차: 4Q26 아반떼 HEV, 투싼, GV90, G80 HEV, 싼타페 FL(2026.12월), G90 
FL 등 출시 예정 
- 북미: 4Q26 투싼, GV80 HEV 출시 및 27년 초 GV90 출시 예상 
- 유럽: 아이오닉3 판매 개시, 4Q26 투싼/베이온 등 주요 모델 출시 예정 
- 중국: 아이오닉 V / 인도: 베이온, 아이오닉 P 등 출시 예정 
- 신차 출시 계획 연도별로 다수 예정. 가격은 원가/경쟁사/소비자 가격 수용성 등을 고
려해 결정

### Page 6

현대차(005380)

Q. 3Q26 법인세비용 증가 원인 및 4Q26 전망 
- 일회성 요인보다는 법인별 손익 믹스 및 적용 세율 차이에 따른 영향 
- 4Q26 법인세 수준은 QoQ 큰 변동 없을 것으로 전망

## Document 287: Anthropic斥资34亿美元采购燃气发电设备，为数据中心供电

Source PDF: all report pdf/Anthropic斥资34亿美元采购燃气发电设备，为数据中心供电.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-11-08
Pages: 2
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Anthropic 于 9 ⽉以 34 亿美元直接接⼿卡特彼勒设备订单，锁定 1.7 吉⽡燃⽓发电机组，成为
AI 史上最⼤单笔发电设备采购。此举绕开公⽤事业中间商，抢占数年前低价下单、近期即
可交付的稀缺产能。这是其 2045 年前逾 8950 亿美元计算基础设施计划的⼀部分。
摘要：
AI 独⻆兽 Anthropic 正以前所未有的⼒度直接介⼊能源基础设施领域。
据英国《⾦融时报》 11 ⽉ 8 ⽇报道，Anthropic 于 9 ⽉下旬斥资 34 亿美元，从得克萨斯州圣安东
尼奥设备供应商 OnePWR Solutions 购得 1.7 吉⽡燃⽓重型发电机组的采购承诺，成为迄今已知
AI 实验室单笔最⼤规模的发电设备采购交易。此次交易还涵盖安装发电机所需的⼯程与施⼯能
⼒，但 Anthropic 并未收购 OnePWR 公司本⾝， OnePWR 员⼯亦未随之转⼊ Anthropic 。
直接采购：绕开中间商抢占稀缺资源
据报道，知情⼈⼠透露， OnePWR 此前已向卡特彼勒（ Caterpillar ）下单订购发电机，但需要融

资⽀持才能完成交易。Anthropic 最初计划以客⼾⾝份向 OnePWR 采购，但最终选择直接接⼿
其在卡特彼勒的设备订单。
这⼀安排使 Anthropic 得以锁定数年前以较低价格下单、近期及未来两年内即可交付的发电机
组，有效规避了当前燃⽓轮机和发电机需求激增、制造交货期延伸⾄数年之后的市场困境。
AI 开发商通常依赖公⽤事业商和专业电⼒公司供电，但随着相关设备⽇趋稀缺，直接掌控⽣产资
源已成为头部科技公司的共同选择。
基础设施布局：近 9000 亿美元的计算承诺
此次采购是 Anthropic 加速掌控⾃⾝运营基础设施这⼀更宏观战略的组成部分。该公司本周披
露，其通过 2045 年的计算承诺规模已接近 9000 亿美元，此前⼀直⾼度依赖合作伙伴在电⼒、芯
⽚、数据中⼼及⼟地⽅⾯提供⽀持，并为锁定产能⽀付了溢价。
直接购置发电机，将使 Anthropic 对这些设施所需电⼒拥有更强的掌控⼒。 Stifel 清洁能源与电⼒
业务董事总经理 Arash Nazhad 表⽰：
值得注意的是，Anthropic 同时也是 SpaceX 的重要客⼾，向其租⽤计算资源的⽉度付款预计到

年底将达到 31 亿美元。
Anthropic 正筹备最早于本⽉启动 IPO ，此次上市有望成为史上规模最⼤的上市交易之⼀，旨在
为其 2045 年前逾 8950 亿美元的计算基础设施⽀出提供资⾦⽀持。  Anthropic 计划最快于 11 ⽉ 9 ⽇
⻢斯克旗下 SpaceX 此前正是通过直接采购发电设备，快速建⽴起⼤规模数据中⼼； xAI 今年早
些时候斥资 20 亿美元购⼊移动式燃⽓轮机；⾕歌⺟公司 Alphabet 则于 2025 年底收购了能源开发
商 Intersect 。
“通过⾃有部分电⼒基础设施并具备内部能⼒，企业相信能够更好地抵御超出⾃⾝控制范围的供
应中断⻛险。 ”
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

## Document 319: Cliff Notes_ managing expectations (2026-11-08)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Cliff Notes_ managing expectations (2026-11-08).pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 10
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

#cliffnotes #rba #australia #inflation #us #fomc #ecb #aud #usd
Cliff Notes: managing expectations
By Ryan Wells, Illiana Jain & Elliot Clarke
11:04 October 23 2026 Share
Key insights from the week that was.
With little domestic data of significance, participants’ attention focused on RBA Deputy Governor
Hauser's fireside chat, which saw an abrupt shift in market expectations of a follow-up rate hike at the
November meeting.
Hauser warned in his remarks that the current inflation outlook was "materially worse" than the RBA's

### Page 2

August forecasts. Three upside inflation risks were characterised as having "crystallised" ahead of the
September meeting – the Middle East, the AI boom, and domestic supply constraints. Hauser noted the
Middle East picture "looks worse" given oil prices are "about a third higher than [the RBA] assumed in
August”. He then positioned AI and data centre developments among an "extraordinary sequence of
positive demand shocks around the world", with "higher AI prices, tech prices feeding through into

CPI". Finally, on domestic capacity pressures, the Deputy Governor noted that "inflation's been coming
in at or a little bit above expectations, despite an apparent easing in the labour market". He went on to
state that the staff are currently assessing "whether [the RBA] need to change [its] judgement on
capacity pressures in the labour market again" – in other words, revise up their assumption for the
NAIRU.
Hauser's comments prompted a material reassessment of market pricing for a follow-up November

hike, from just a 20% chance at the end of last week to a 50/50 proposition today ahead of next week's
Q3 CPI. For that release, we are forecasting a 1.3% (4.1%yr) lift in headline inflation and a 0.9 (3.5%yr)
increase in trimmed mean inflation. Our preview provides more detail behind the component mix for Q3
as well as our upward revisions to the preliminary Q4 forecast. Provided inflation prints broadly in line
with our expectations, the RBA is likely to hike by 25bp at the November meeting.

Across in New Zealand, consumer prices rose 0.7% in Q3 and annual inflation eased from 4.1%yr to
3.7%yr. Under the surface of this ‘as expected’ read though, several closely watched measures of core
inflation took a step higher to now be at or slightly above the top of the RBNZ’s target band. Our NZ
economics team consequently expect the RBNZ will raise the OCR 25bps to 3.00% at its October
policy review, while retaining a 4.00% terminal OCR forecast for September 2027.

Earlier in the week, China's economy surprised to the upside in Q3, GDP growth accelerating from
0.9%qtr to 1.2%qtr, lifting annual growth from 4.3%yr to 4.5%yr – keeping authorities on track to
achieve their 4.5-5.0% full-year growth objective.
While annual retail sales growth improved in September, rising from 0.4%yr to 1.1%yr, the current pace
of growth is only a quarter of the gain seen just a year ago and very weak versus history. Consumer
spending continues to be constrained by weak confidence, particularly with respect to employment

prospects and wealth. On housing wealth, five years into the property downturn, house prices continue
to decline month-on-month and investment remains in a deep downturn, -20.6%ytd.
Total fixed asset investment has been detracting from annual GDP growth since April owing to a lull in
high-tech investment, a lack of confidence in future demand amongst domestically oriented businesses
as well as property’s malaise. For aggregate growth to hold up through 2027 and 2028 as the

contribution from net exports moderates, broad-based stimulus in scale will prove necessary. Expect
recent targeted support measures to be broadened and given much greater scale in coming months.
Finally, in the UK, inflation accelerated from 3.1%yr to 3.5%yr in September, exceeding both market
expectations and the Bank of England's July forecast. The surprise was largely driven by energy, fuel
prices rising 5.4%mth. However, core inflation also edged up from 2.6%yr to 2.8%yr as services

inflation increased from 3.4%yr to 3.7%yr. While not an immediate concern inflation, the labour market
showing resilience, the ILO unemployment rate falling from 4.9% to 4.7% in August, suggests demand

### Page 3

Related articles
risks for inflation will need close monitoring as well as supply-side concerns.
Next week will see the focus once again return to the US as the FOMC meet and US Q3 GDP is
released. For both events, the implications for risks to the outlook will be the focus. The week after, the
US mid-term elections are due.

## Document 339: EcoNews of 2 November 2026 (2026-11-02)

Source PDF: all report pdf/EcoNews of 2 November 2026 (2026-11-02).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-11-02
Pages: 2
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

The bank
for a changing
world
5
economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoWeek 26.40 / 2 November 2026ECONEWS
Find out more in our scenario and forecasts
ADVANCED ECONOMIES
In brief. Data releases confirmed resilient to strong growth and
above-forecast inflation in most advanced economies, with France lag-
ging behind and earning a rating outlook downgrade from Fitch as a
result. Fed, ECB and BoJ remained on hold, as expected, and on track
for December hikes. Oil continues to fluctuate between escalation fears

and hopes for a return to the negotiating table. The tech sell-off in
equity markets intensified despite strong earnings, as markets balk at
large capex plans. This helped bond yields ease from recent highs.
EUROZONE
Resilience confirmed despite tightening credit. GDP expanded by a
stronger-than-expected 0.4% q/q in Q3 (0.2% ex-Ireland), with Spain,
Germany and the Netherlands contributing to the bulk of non-Irish
growth. We increase our growth forecast to 1.6% in 2027 (+0.1pp),

driven by further improving sentiment in manufacturing. Bank lending
conditions tightened further in Q3 for businesses, especially SMEs,
driven by France and Germany, while corporate and housing-loan de -
mand weakened. The ECB kept its deposit rate at 2.5% and signaled it
was firmly on track for a (likely final) December hike. President Lagarde
also confirmed she would step down early, on February 7, setting off
the succession race. EU leaders are expected to decide jointly on the

three Board members departing between January and May (President,
chief economist, and market operations), likely at their mid-December
meeting. Official candidates to succeed Lagarde are BIS Head Pablo
Hernandez de Cos and former Dutch central bank Governor Klaas Knot.
To watch next week: October flash CPI (Wednesday).
France: Another growth setback, with silver linings
GDP contracted by 0.1% q/q in Q3 as a sharp drop in aeronauti -
cal production hit exports and investment. However, services out -

put grew by a robust 0.3% q/q, and household consumption also
grew more strongly than forecasted (+0.4%). While 2026 will ine -
vitably be knocked down (to 0.3%), we forecast 0.8% for 2027.
Nevertheless, Fitch issued an unscheduled rating outlook downgrade
to negative territory, on grounds that fiscal, growth and ad -
justment prospects have deteriorated since its August review.
A new review could be concluded as early as February. Harmonised
inflation accelerated to 3.8% y/y in October, with both energy and

non-energy contributing. Meanwhile, producer price inflation accele -
rated to 8.2% y/y in September. Insee’s index of household confidence
fell to 80, matching its 2013 all-time low, driven by a decline in ex -
pectations of its own financial situation. Private payroll employment
declined by 26,000 over Q3 (-0.1%). However, in previous quarters, the
decline in payroll employment was more than offset by the increase in
self-employment (not released until November 27).

To watch next week: September industrial production (Thursday); Sep-
tember foreign trade (Friday).
Germany: Growth holds up as inflation slows
GDP grew by 0.2% q/q in Q3, with consumption rising slightly but in -
vestment declining and export growth slowing. HICP inflation fell from
3.3% to 2.8% in October following energy-tax cuts and fuel discounts,
but core inflation also slowed, to 2.1%. Business confidence (Ifo survey)
reached its highest level in more than three years, led by manufactu -

ring, which is back in positive territory for the first time since 2023.
By contrast, consumer confidence deteriorated to its lowest since May,
due to growing concerns about inflation, which are weighing on consu-
mers’ spending intentions.
To watch next week: September retail sales (Monday); September fac-
tory orders (Thursday); September industrial production (Friday).
Italy: Stagnant growth meets energy-driven inflation surge
GDP was flat in Q3, missing expectations, as positive domestic demand

was offset by net exports. Economic sentiment improved, but HICP in -
flation jumped from 4.2% to 5.6% in October, its highest level since
September 2023, mainly because of regulated energy prices; core in -
flation also rose, to a more moderate 2%. More positively, economic
sentiment rose to 100.2 in October 2026 (from 99.4 in September),
despite the Eurozone decline. The improvement was driven by services
and construction. The Italian cabinet approved a budget focused on

middle-class tax cuts, financed partly by levies on banks, energy and
gambling companies. x
UNITED KINGDOM
A cautious budget buys breathing space. The budget offered 98 small
cost-of-living alleviation measures and £4.1 billion in household ener-
gy cost relief, while only modestly increasing taxes, including a tem -
porary rise in the bank surcharge. Its short-term stance is slightly ex -
pansionary, while major structural decisions on defence, pensions and

welfare have been postponed. The official budget watchdog, OBR, cut
its 2027 growth forecast to 1%, and firms’ inflation expectations conti-
nued to rise. UK gilts outperformed and equities advanced after the
budget. GBP rose to a six-week high against the dollar and a 19-month
high versus the euro as markets price a near-certain Bank of England
rate hike (to 4%) at this week meeting. We agree but, unlike market
pricing, we do not expect it to hike further.

To watch next week: Bank of England interest-rate decision (Thursday).
UNITED STATES
Fed awaiting more information despite strong growth. The Federal Re-
serve held its target range at 3.75–4.0%, with two members voting for a
25 bp increase, as Chair Warsh highlighted stronger growth, favourable
employment conditions and inflation that remains too high. Q3 GDP
accelerated to 3.4% annualised, powered by resilient consumption and
a 12.7% jump in business investment. Despite jobless claims remaining

near multi-decade lows, compensation costs adjusted for inflation de-
clined 0.4%, marking the second consecutive quarter in which labour
cost growth trailed price increases.
To watch next week: Midterm elections (Tuesday); October ISM ma -
nufacturing (Monday) and non-manufacturing (Wednesday); Q3 non-
farm productivity (Thursday); October non-farm payrolls (Friday).
JAPAN
BoJ on hold with underlying inflation “consistent with the 2% tar -
get”. The Bank of Japan kept its policy rate at 1.25%, with one dissent

in favour of a hike. Its outlook judged underlying inflation to be
consistent with the 2% target, a major shift in its assessment, although
Governor Ueda mentioned risks on both sides in his remarks, stres -
sing in particular the risk of inflation overshooting the target. It signals
continued gradual monetary adjustment, with a next hike expected in
December. Alongside the rate hold, the BOJ announced it would near -
ly triple the pace of ETF sales starting in January, to approximately

JPY 860 billion per year. Japanese equities shrugged off the announ -
cement.
To watch next week: Minutes of the September Bank of Japan meeting
(Thursday); September wage growth (Friday).

### Page 2

6
economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoWeek 26.40 / 2 November 2026

EMERGING ECONOMIES
ASIA
China: Monetary policy reform and no growth signals
The PBOC disclosed a 1.25% overnight reverse-repo rate and an -
nounced that the overnight rate would become its principal policy
lever, starting 2nd November. It also pledged to develop cross-border
renminbi payments and introduced a liquidity backstop for non-bank

financial institutions. The 5th Plenum of the 20 th Central Committee of
the CCP focused on party discipline and crackdown on corrupt cadres,
without broader policy signals. Chinese equities had their worst month
since 2016, falling 8.5% in October, as doubts over AI earnings deepe -
ned investor pessimism. The PMIs published on 30 th October suggest
economic activity is losing momentum; in the manufacturing sector,
the RatingDog PMI fell slightly to 52 (from 52.1) while the official PMI

fell more sharply and dropped below 50 (49.3 vs. 50.1 in the previous
month).
South Korea and Taiwan: AI boom lifts growth even higher
In Korea, GDP grew by 1.0% q/q and 3.4% y/y in Q3, supported by boo -
ming semiconductor exports linked to global AI demand. The expansion
was uneven, however, as private-consumption growth slowed marke -
dly. In Taiwan, GDP surged by 3.2% q/q and 14.4% y/y in Q3, beating
expectations and accelerating from the previous quarter as technology

firms expanded capacity to meet strong demand.
EMEA
Poland: Lower inflation gives monetary policy some room
Headline inflation declined from 4.0% to 3.5% in October as electricity,
gas and transport fuel prices rose more slowly, helped by a temporary
fuel-price cap. Rates are likely to remain unchanged in the near term,
although renewed zloty weakness means a hike cannot be ruled out.
Saudi Arabia: Reserves fall as regional pressures intensify
Official reserve assets declined by 4.4% m/m in September to

SAR 1.75 tn, their lowest level since December, with foreign-currency
assets recording particularly sharp monthly and annual declines.
Türkiye: Pressure on lira and FX reserves
The lira headed for its largest monthly fall in 15 months and cen -
tral-bank reserves came under renewed pressure. This is the conse -
quence of the warning by MSCI that Türkiye could lose its emer -
ging-market status if measures to improve transparency and market
supervision following the Fund scandal are deemed insufficient.

LATIN AMERICA
Brazil’s post-election rout: stocks and real plunge, yields surge. Bra -
zilian markets, expecting a Flavio Bolsonaro victory, sold off sharply
after Luiz Inacio Lula da Silva won a fourth presidential term. The Ibo-
vespa fell 14% Monday — its largest daily drop since COVID-19 — and
ended the week down 18%. The real fell over 11% intraday and ended
the week down 8% against the euro. Meanwhile the yield on Brazil’s
10-year government bonds rose 250 bp to 15.5%, reaching its highest

level in over a decade. The selloff reflects fiscal concerns over pledges
to raise the minimum wage and Bolsa familia benefits and to offer free
public transport on weekends and bank holidays. Finance Minister Da-
rio Durigan’s pledge to seek a higher 2027 primary surplus, without a
new target, did little to reassure markets.
COMMODITIES
The oil market continues to fluctuate between escalation fears and
hopes for a return to the negotiating table. Brent prices were down by

5% over the week, reaching USD 109/b on Monday. Tensions on physical
supply continue to decline. Ditto for diesel crack spreads. On the back
of geopolitical tensions, lower-than-requested EU stock level and spe-
culation over a cold snap in Europe in November, European gas prices
(TTF) recorded another strong weekly rise and have breached the
EUR 100/MWh for the first time since Dec 2022). The Asian gas bench-
mark (JKM) is following the same trend but at a slower pace (4.6% w/w

to USD 32 /MBtu, equivalent to around EUR 96.3 /MWh).
TO WATCH NEXT WEEK
Global: Manufacturing PMIs for EM and final DM prints (Monday); final
services and composite PMIs (Wednesday).
y/y = year-on-year
m/m = month-on-month
bp = basis point
pp = percentage point
mb = million barrels
MBtu = Million British Thermal Units

## Document 352: Following the oil shock, El Niño threatens to hurt macroeconomic resilience in Sub-Saharan Africa further (2026-10-21)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Following the oil shock, El Niño threatens to hurt macroeconomic resilience in Sub-Saharan Africa further (2026-10-21).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-10-21
Pages: 3
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

21 October 2026
The bank
for a changing
world
FOLLOWING THE OIL SHOCK, EL NIÑO THREATENS TO HURT MACROECONOMIC RESILIENCE IN SUB-SAHARAN
AFRICA FURTHER
Lucas Plé
Seven months after the start of the war in the Middle East, which has led to a sharp rise in oil prices, sub-Saharan Africa is holding up well
overall. The deterioration in external accounts and the rise in inflation are more contained than in 2022, during the last oil shock. However,
this resilience is fragile and is eroding as the conflict drags on. Furthermore, an exceptional El Niño event could significantly worsen the

macroeconomic environment in 2027 1.
Economic growth remains robust
For countries with available data, GDP growth held up well in the first half of 2026 2. In mid-October, the IMF revised its regional forecast for
the full year upwards to 4.5% (+0.2 percentage points compared with April), in line with the performance for 2025.
1 El Niño 2026–2027: multiple pressures on prices and supplies
2 1.4% year-on-year (y/y) in South Africa, 4.2% in Nigeria, 5.1% in Kenya, 7% in Angola, 7.3% in Ghana.

Sub-Saharan Africa is highly vulnerable to climate shocks via the agricultural commodities channel

### Page 2

2
The bank for
a changing
world
Chart of the Week / 21 October 2026 economic-research.bnpparibas.com
A mitigated shock on terms of trade
High prices for export commodities (gold, copper, platinum) have helped to cushion the impact on terms of trade and the external
accounts of hydrocarbon-importing countries. For the 10 countries in our sample (see heatmap), the depreciation of their currencies
against the US dollar since last February has been moderate overall (2.3% on average).

Inflation has started to rise again, but it is still being contained so far
In the ten countries in our sample, although inflation is rising again, it is lower year-on-year for the first eight months of 2026 than it was
for the same period in 2022. This is due to:
1. Monetary policies that were still restrictive before the conflict started;
2. Fuel subsidies;
3.   Macroeconomic reforms implemented in recent years (a return to orthodox monetary policy in Nigeria, and efforts towards fiscal

consolidation in many countries);
4. Moderate currency depreciation since February.
As a result, few central banks have tightened their monetary policy since February (South Africa, Uganda and Tanzania).
However, this resilience is still fragile…
On the one hand, governments that have chosen to cap fuel price rises are seeing their public finances deteriorate. In Senegal, energy
subsidies will go above 3% of GDP in 2026, which is unsustainable. On the other hand, the region’s external accounts are exposed to

volatile global commodity prices and tighter international financing conditions.
… and the macroeconomic environment could quickly worsen in 2027, due to an exceptional El Niño event
The region is particularly vulnerable to climate shocks via the agricultural commodities channel. Given agriculture’s high share in its GDP ,
sub-Saharan Africa is more exposed to downside risks to growth than Latin America, Asia or Central Europe (1 st column of the heatmap).

It is also more exposed than other regions of the world to the inflationary effect of rising food prices, given their higher share in the
consumer basket (2 nd column). Finally, even though its dependence on food imports is lower than in Asia and Europe (3 rd column), the
region is highly vulnerable to rising international food commodity prices due to its fragile external accounts. For several countries in our
sample, foreign exchange reserves cover less than four months’ worth of imports of goods and services (Uganda, Tanzania, Ghana and

Zambia), leaving little room for manoeuvre for absorbing a further external shock.
In addition to shocks via the agricultural commodities channel, other localised risks must be considered. In Southern Africa, El Niño is
expected to intensify droughts and threaten electricity generation as a result, particularly in Zambia (where hydropower accounts
for nearly 80% of domestic electricity generation). In East Africa, the increased flooding that El Niño is expected to cause will threaten

infrastructure – particularly logistics infrastructure – which is already less efficient than the global average 3. These two sources of
vulnerability could also increase pressure on growth, prices and the external accounts.
In conclusion, if oil prices remain high and El Niño is exceptionally severe, the region will struggle to absorb this double shock in 2027.
Despite their status as net exporters of hydrocarbons, Nigeria and Angola are also exposed to a deterioration in the macroeconomic

environment in 2027. Only South Africa appears to be better positioned to absorb the shock.
lucas.ple@bnpparibas.com
3 See the World Bank’s Logistics Performance Index.

## Document 364: Gridlock Won & Markets Cheer

Source PDF: all report pdf/Gridlock Won & Markets Cheer.pdf
Report source: researchmail轉寄
Date: 2026-11-05
Pages: 8
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

From:    Yardeni QuickTakes <yardeni-research@ghost.io>
Subject: Gridlock Won & Markets Cheer
Date:    6 hours ago
Gridlock Won & Markets Cheer

YARDENI QUICKTAKES
Gridlock Won & Markets Cheer
By Ed Yardeni & Elias Griepentrog • 4 Nov 2026
View in browser

I. Why Wall Street Is Cheering the Blue Sweep
Ahead of Tuesday's midterm elections, we warned that a Democratic
sweep of Congress could deliver a cold shower to Wall Street. It still could,
but it didn't do so today. Democrats captured both chambers of Congress,
flipping seven Republican-held Senate seats, including historic victories in
Texas, Kansas, and South Carolina. Financial markets put a positive spin
on the blue wave.
Oil prices fell on hopes that the US and Iran are more likely now to resolve
their war diplomatically. If so, then the outlook for inflation will improve. So
the Fed might not have to raise the federal funds rate as much. The

### Page 2

federal deficit might not widen further if Democrats refuse to increase
defense spending, while President Donald Trump vetoes their attempts to
provide Medicare for all. If Democrats slow the AI buildout, hyperscalers'
profits and free cash flow will increase. Fewer AI-related bond issues
would also help keep a lid on bond yields.
Initially, Nasdaq-100 futures plunged as much as 2.4% overnight, while
S&P 500 futures fell 1.4%. But the selloff reversed after Trump struck a

surprisingly conciliatory tone, congratulating Democrats and saying he
was willing to work with them.
That is when the cheer on Wall Street started. The Dow surged 3.0%
today, the strongest post-midterm rally since 1982. The S&P 500 jumped
1.6%, its best session in three months. The Nasdaq-100 advanced 0.9%.
The relief rally extended beyond equities. The 10-year Treasury yield fell
to 4.90%, back in what we have called the "old normal" range of
4.00%-5.00% (chart).

The dollar index (DXY) fell more than 1.5%, its biggest decline since
September. Gold rallied back above $4,000 an ounce. It might be finally
bottoming.

### Page 3

de-escalation in the Middle East
Brent crude plunged 8.7% to $94.62 a barrel amid hopes of Middle East
de-escalation. The nearby futures price of gasoline plunged to $2.73
(chart).
Investors may be warming up to the idea that some constraints on the AI
buildout could be healthy, as we have been arguing recently. Democratic
victories in key data center states, including Texas and Georgia, raise the
prospect of tighter construction restrictions amid concerns about electricity

and water consumption.
Hyperscalers rallied today, with Amazon and Microsoft gaining more than
6%, as capital discipline could ease concerns about AI investment returns
and improve free cash flow (chart). AI infrastructure providers struggled,

### Page 4

however. The PHLX Semiconductor Index fell 3.1% amid fears of weaker
demand from a slower buildout. We may be entering an AI Goldilocks
zone, with enough restraint to take some air out of the bubble without
derailing the boom.
II. The US Economy Continues To Perform Well
The economy and, therefore, earnings remain fundamentally resilient,
which continues to support the bull market. With the midterms behind us
and political uncertainty receding, investors can refocus on these strong

fundamentals. Here are the highlights of today's upbeat economic
indicators:
(1) ISM Services PMI. The ISM-NM PMI edged down modestly to 54.5 in
October, marking its 28th consecutive month of expansion (chart).
Underlying demand remained solid, with new orders and business activity
both comfortably above the 50.0 expansion threshold. Encouragingly, the
employment index rose from 50.1 to 51.4, marking its second consecutive
month of expansion.

### Page 5

Inflation is still a concern. The ISM prices-paid indexes remained elevated
in October (chart).
(2) Labor market. The average of the ISM employment indexes rose to
51.9 in October, its highest reading in roughly three years (chart). ADP
reported an 86,000 increase in private payrolls in October, beating
consensus expectations of 67,000.

### Page 6

(3) Trade balance. The latest trade data point to resilient US and global
economic growth. Merchandise imports rose 2.4% m/m in September to a
record $3.44 trillion annualized, while exports increased 2.5% m/m to
$2.09 trillion. The merchandise trade deficit widened to $1.35 trillion
annualized (chart).
Nominal and real US merchandise exports plus imports rose 21.1% y/y
and 10.1%, respectively, in September (chart). That suggests global trade
remains firmly in expansion territory and is consistent with strong global

economic growth.

### Page 7

Selected technology imports reached a record $61.3 billion in September,
reflecting the AI buildout’s heavy reliance on imported semiconductors,
servers, and other hardware (chart).
💡

leave comments or questions at the end of our QuickTakes, and it
may be featured in the next "Just Ask Dr. Ed!"

### Page 8

clause.

2
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
TODAY’S INSIGHTS
Financial Markets
Markets were generally buoyed overnight, with Anthropic’s
IPO filing and Meta’s US$25bn equity offering closing five
times oversubscribed adding momentum back into the
AI trade, while softer oil prices provided further support.

Attention now turns to a series of key economic developments,
with Australian CPI due today alongside policy decisions and
accompanying guidance from the RBNZ, BoC and FOMC.
• US equities moved higher, supported by AI-related
stocks. The S&P 500 gained 0.8%, while the NASDAQ
outperformed, rising 1.2%, and the Dow Jones was
unchanged. European markets were mixed, with the Euro
Stoxx 50 up 0.8%, while the FTSE 100 fell 0.8% ahead of
the UK Budget.
• Tech-heavy Asian markets were buoyed as recent headlines

helped ease concerns around AI financing. The KOSPI led
gains, rising 3.1%, while the Nikkei 225 also performed
strongly, up 1.7%. The Hang Seng diverged from regional
peers, falling 0.7%. Locally, the ASX 200 gained 0.5%.
• US Treasury yields were little changed, with the 2-year
yield rising 1bp and the 10-year yield unchanged. Futures
markets remain cautious about pricing further Fed tightening
in 2026, with the probability of a hike at today’s FOMC

meeting sitting at just 7%, taking the implied year-end fed
funds rate to 4.10%.
• Australian government bond yields were little changed
ahead of today’s CPI release, with the 3-year yield rising
1bp and the 10-year yield falling 1bp. Swap markets remain
cautious around additional RBA tightening in 2026, with the
probability of a November rate hike priced at 62%, taking
the implied year-end cash rate to 4.80%.
• Pre-FOMC positioning supported the US dollar, with

the DXY up 0.3%. The AUD outperformed other major
currencies, rising 0.2% against the greenback to 0.7028 and
also gaining across the major crosses. The euro and yen both
fell 0.3% against the USD, while the sterling declined 0.2%.
• Oil prices declined on hopes that diplomacy could avert a
renewed escalation in the US-Iran conflict. Brent fell 1.6%
to US$114/bbl, while WTI declined 1.9% to US$99.9/bbl.
Dated Brent traded at US$121/bbl. Gold rose 1.1% ahead

of today’s FOMC meeting, while positive risk sentiment
supported base metals, with copper up 1.0% and aluminium
gaining 1.5%. Iron ore fell 1.1%, remaining below US$90/t
amid persistent concerns around global demand.
International Data
US durable goods orders rose 1.8% in September after a 0.1%
gain in August. Growth for core orders (non defence, ex aircraft)
was more modest in September at 0.5% but follows a 1.6% rise
in August.
US S&P Cotality Case Shiller house prices rose a further 0.3%

in August, accelerating annual growth at the margin to 2.7%yr.
US new home sales fell 0.6% in September following a
downwardly revised 5.1% gain (prev 6.4%) in August. Sales are
currently near the average of the past 5 years.
US Conference Board consumer confidence jumped from 84.2
to 91.7 in October on an improvement in current conditions
and expectations. While still below average, this measure of
consumer confidence continues to outperform the University of

Michigan survey, held up by the robust strength of the labour
market. Responses to the Michigan measure instead look to be
focused more on cost of living.
The Richmond Fed manufacturing index was unchanged at -2
in October.
Local Data
Our New Zealand team published their 2026 General Election
preview, outlining the major parties’ key policies (see here).

## Document 465: Westpac NZ Retail Spending Pulse, October 2026 (2026-10-01)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Westpac NZ Retail Spending Pulse, October 2026 (2026-10-01).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-10-01
Pages: 7
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

RETAIL
SPENDING
PULSE
6 November 2026

### Page 2

Westpac New Zealand Economics
2
Satish Ranchhod, Senior Economist | +64 9 336 5668 | +64 21 710 852 | satish.ranchhod@westpac.co.nz      Connect with us
Discretionary spending flat as high fuel
prices bite
• Per-person spending on Westpac issued credit and debit cards was up 1.2% in October and is 7.8%
higher than this time last year.
October spending.
• October’s rise in spending was mainly related to the sharp increase in petrol prices, with the average

price for 91-unleaded around the country climbing to a record high of over $3.60/ltr. That saw
spending on fuel rise 14% over the month.
• Those higher fuel prices, along with other cost of living pressures, have put the brakes on other
purchases. Spending in discretionary areas was down 0.4% in October and has essentially been
tracking sideways since May. There’s been notable softness in spending on travel and household
furnishings. However, spending in restaurants has been holding up.

Annual growth.
• While spending has certainly been constrained by the rise in living costs since the start of this year,
spending levels are still well above those we saw in 2025. And that’s not just because of high fuel
prices, with spending in discretionary areas also up compared to this time last year.
• That’s been supported by factors such as the drop in borrowing costs over the past year and
strength in export earnings. The boost from those factors is helping to balance the impacts of

factors such as softness in the labour market and high fuel prices. However, as we highlight over the
page, the strength in spending has been uneven across the country.

3
Satish Ranchhod, Senior Economist | +64 9 336 5668 | +64 21 710 852 | satish.ranchhod@westpac.co.nz      Connect with us
Regional breakdown
• As has been the case for some time, spending growth has been strongest in Southland. Other parts
of the country with strong rural backbones like Canterbury and Northland have also continued to
see solid gains.
• Spending growth remains more modest in Auckland. However, we expect that spending in Auckland

will take a step higher next month, with hospitality spending in particular likely to be boosted by
events like the Indian cricket tour, Diwali and Auckland Marathon.
• In a notable move, Wellington has shifted up the ranks and now sits in the middle of the pack.
Wellingtonians are no doubt holding their breath ahead of Saturday’s tight election and many will be
stocking up for the traditional election night barbecue and watch party.*

ASIA MONTHLY 2026 年 10 月（No.307）
JAPAN RESEARCH INSTITUTE
1
中国の成長分野における赤字企業の増加
中国では、 赤字企業が増加している。 とりわけ、 成長分野における増加が顕著である。 この要因の一つは、
し烈な企業間競争による選別である。また、赤字でも潜在力が評価される次世の成長企業も多い。
■成長分野における赤字企業の増加
近年、中国では成長分野における赤字企業の
増加が顕著である。上場企業数に占める赤字企
業の比率（赤字企業比率）は2019年の 9.6％か
ら 2025年に 26.9％へ急上昇している （右上図）。
赤字企業の業種分布をみると、2019～22 年は、
新型コロナウイルス対策として実施された厳格
な外出規制や不動産不況を受けて、飲食・宿泊
業上場企業（以下、上場企業を省略）や不動産
業など非製造業を中心に増加した。 一方で、2022
～25 年には、情報通信機器・電子部品・デバイ
ス、はん用・生産用・業務用機械、電気機械、

化学工業で赤字企業が急増している。これら 4
分野だけで全体の上昇分の約6割を説明できる。
これらの分野は中国製造業の代表的な成長分
野である。実際、この 4 分野の付加価値額の増
加率は、いずれも中国製造業の平均値を上回る。
中国政府が重点的に育成してきたハイテク製造
業も、 これらの分野に多数含まれる。 たとえば、
5G基地局や半導体は、 情報通信機器・電子部品・
デバイスに分類される。ロボットは、はん用・
生産用・業務用機械に含まれる。太陽電池や蓄
電池は電気機械、バイオ医薬品や有機化学素材
は化学工業に属している。
情報通信機器・電子部品・デバイス製造業の
近年の動きをみると、2019年頃から世界的に通信規格が4G（LTE）から 5G サービスへの移行
がみられ、2022年 11 月に ChatGPTが公開された。これらが、同分野の成長の追い風となって
いる。 足元では、 一般の消費者や企業がAIサービスを日常的に利用できることが示されたことで、
AI向け端末や半導体、 データセンターの需要が急増している。 こうしたなか、 多くの企業が開発・

製造・販売の強化に注力しており、 同分野の付加価値額は2022年の7,800億元から2025年に9,100
億元へと増加し続けている。その一方で、赤字企業数は同期間に113社から 198社へ増加し、赤
字企業比率は17.2％から 30.1％へと急上昇している（右下図）。
■企業間競争を通じた選別
成長分野でありながら赤字企業が増加している理由の一つは、し烈な企業間競争である。市場
シェアを巡って、設備投資競争や価格競争が過熱している。情報通信機器・電子部品・デバイス
主任研究員 関 辰一
(seki.shinichi@jri.co.jp）

JAPAN RESEARCH INSTITUTE
2
製造業についてみると、2022年から 2025年の
期間における固定資産残高の増加率（＋35.4％）
は、付加価値額の増加率（＋16.9％）を大きく
上回る。これは、供給能力が需要を上回るベー
スで拡大していることを示している。また、消
費者物価指数（CPI）は同期間に＋0.4％とほぼ
横ばい、そのうちサービス価格は賃金上昇など
を反映して同＋2.2％上昇している一方で、財
（モノ）の価格は製造業における供給超過によ
り同▲0.7％下落している。情報通信機器価格の
下落幅は▲3.5％と財全体と比べても大きい。
企業間競争を通じた選別が進んだ結果、利益
は競争力の高い少数企業に集中している。情報通信機器・電子部品・デバイス製造業の利益総額
に占めるトップ1％企業の利益の割合が2022年の 24.0％から 2025年に 43.3％へと、 大きく上昇
している（右上図）。有力企業は「国内チャンピオン企業」であると同時に「グローバル・チャ

ンピオン企業」としてのポジションを形成しつつある。
■赤字でも潜在力が評価される次代の成長企業
ただし、赤字企業を敗者としてひとくくりにすることはできない。近年、世界的には赤字であ
っても成長性が高い企業に対する投資家の関心が高まっており、中国においても同様な赤字企業
が少なからず存在している。
情報通信機器・電子部品・デバイス製造業に
ついてみると、赤字企業の2 社のうち 1 社は投
資家が高い関心を示している企業である。上海
総合株価指数は、2023年初の 3,089ポイントか
ら2025年末に3,969ポイントへと＋28.5％上昇
した。2025年の赤字企業198社のうち 2023年
時点で上場していなかった 20 社を除くと 178
社となる。この178社のうち 96社（53. 9％）
の当該期間における株価上昇率は、上海総合株
価指数の上昇率を上回る（右下図）。
この相対株価パフォーマンスの良い 96 社の
特徴の一つは、研究開発や無形資産への投資に
積極的である点である。96社の 2022年の R&D

集約度（研究開発投資／売上高）の平均値は
9.6％、無形資産残高の対付加価値額比率も60.5％に達している。無形資産には、コンピュータの
ソフトウェア、特許、著作権、顧客や仕入れ先との関係などが含まれる。これに対し、相対株価
パフォーマンスの悪い赤字企業82 社の R&D 集約度は 6.5％、無形資産残高の対付加価値額比率
は 38.9％にとどまる。
このように、中国の成長分野における赤字企業の増加は顕著であり、その背景には企業間競争
による選別だけでなく、潜在力が評価される次代の成長企業が生まれていることも大きい。

気候変動が高めるアジアの成長停滞リスク
気候変動は、労働生産性や資本蓄積の停滞を通じて ASEAN・インドの成長停滞リスクを高め得る。その
軽減・回避に向け、官民による「適応投資」を促す事業環境と制度の整備が急務である。
■アジアで深刻化する大雨・洪水被害
アジアでは近年、 人的被害を伴う大規模な大雨
や洪水が頻発している（右上表）。記憶に新しい
のは、本年 8月にネパールと中国の国境付近で発
生した土石流であり、本稿執筆時点では死者・行
方不明者が数千人に上る大災害となっている。ま
た、昨年11月にも東南アジア・南アジアで記録
的な豪雨が発生し、 インドネシアとスリランカを
中心に多数の犠牲者が出た。
こうした洪水・大雨のリスクを高める要因の一
つが、気候変動である。気温が上昇すると大気に
含まれる水蒸気量が増えるため、 地球温暖化は豪
雨の激甚化をもたらし得る。 こうしたリスクはア

ジア全体に共通するものだが、 その影響の受け方
は地域によって異なる。なかでもASEAN・イン
ドは、モンスーン（季節風）の影響を強く受け、
雨季に降水が集中する地域が多い。 こうした地域
では、 温暖化によりモンスーンがもたらす大雨をさらに強め、 洪水や土砂災害のリスクを高める。
■気候変動が遠ざける「中所得国の罠」からの脱却
気候変動は、自然災害による人的被害をもたらすのみならず、様々な経路を通じてASEAN・
インド各国の長期的な経済成長力を損なう可能性がある。ADB［2024］は、気候変動がアジア各
国の経済を長期的にどの程度下押しするかについて、定量的な分析を行った。この分析は、IPCC
の「温室効果ガス高排出シナリオ（SSP5-8.5）」に基づき、産業革命以前と比較して2100 年ま
でに地球の平均気温が4.7℃上昇すると仮定したうえで、気候変動がなかった場合の実質GDPと
の差分を推計したものである。推計結果は、気候変動が招く海水面上昇、洪水、熱波などの事象
が、労働生産性の低下や資本蓄積の停滞、消費の

下押しを通じて経済成長に及ぼす長期的な影響
を示している。
結果を見ると、各国・地域とも気候変動がなか
った場合と比べた実質GDPの下押し幅は、2035
年、2050年、2070年と時間の経過とともに拡大
していくことがわかる （右下図）。また、ASEAN・
インドの下押し幅は、2050年時点で▲15.0～▲
8.8％、2070年時点で▲30.2～▲18.1％と、中国
（2050 年：▲4.8％、2070 年：▲10.3％）や中
主任研究員 細井 友洋
(hosoi.tomohiro@jri.co.jp）

▲3 0

JAPAN RESEARCH INSTITUTE
4
央アジア諸国（2050年：▲1.7％、2070 年：▲
3.8％）に比べて大きい。ASEAN・インドはアジ
アのなかでも気候変動による経済停滞リスクが
相対的に大きい地域であると言えよう。
このことは、ASEAN・インドが直面する「中
所得国の罠」 からの脱却を一段と難しくするおそ
れがある。これらの地域では、所得水準は上昇し
ているものの、 高所得国の基準にはなお距離があ
る（右上図）。1990年代またはそれ以前に中所
得国となったインドネシア、マレーシア、フィリ
ピン、タイは、長らく高所得国入りを果たせてい
ない。また、2000 年代以降に中所得国となった
ベトナムとインドも、 中国がかつて経験したよう
な高成長軌道には乗れていない。気候変動は、気
温上昇による労働生産性の停滞や災害の頻発化
による投資抑制などを通じて、ASEAN・インド
の所得向上をさらに遅らせる要因となり得る。

■「適応投資」の加速に向けた環境整備が重要
こうした気候変動による成長下押しを緩和す
るには、官民による「適応投資」の拡大が不可欠
である。具体的には、気温上昇に伴う労働生産性
の低下を和らげる労働環境の改善や省力化・省人化、海水面上昇・洪水による生産設備への被害
を防ぐインフラ整備・設備強靭化などが挙げられる。もっとも、ASEAN・インドは、こうした適
応投資を推進する官民の基盤が十分に整っているとは言いがたい。
米ノートルダム大学は「グローバル適応イニシアティブ（ND-GAIN）」のなかで、投資を活用
して気候変動への適応につなげる国の能力を「適応準備度」として指標化しており、経済、ガバ
ナンス、社会の 3 項目から評価を行っている。それぞれ、①民間企業が適応投資を行いやすい事
業環境が準備されているか、②官民の投資を支える制度や政治の実効性が確保されているか、③
投資を促進するソフト・ハード面での社会インフラが整っているか、を評価するものである。
2024年の適応準備度を見ると、ASEAN・インドの世界順位は総じて中位～下位圏にとどまり、

先進国との差が大きい（右下表）。とりわけ、フィリピン、インド、タイは100位圏外に位置し、
適応投資の推進力が弱いことがうかがえる。内訳を見ると、とくに経済面とガバナンス面に問題
がみられる。インドとタイの経済準備度はそれぞれ113 位、124 位と際立って低く、ガバナンス
準備度もマレーシアを除く5 カ国で 100 位圏外にとどまる。これらを踏まえると、インドとタイ
ではとくに、民間企業による適応投資の加速に向けて、必要資金を内外から調達しやすくする金
融環境整備、新事業立ち上げを促進する規制の緩和、人材を円滑に確保できる労働市場改革など
を急ぐ必要がある。加えて、地域全体として、政策の安定性向上や法制度の実効性確保、汚職の
抑制など、官民投資を支える制度・政治の信頼性を高めることも重要である。
（参考文献）

JAPAN RESEARCH INSTITUTE
5
韓 国  景気回復の勢い、年後半は鈍化
■AI需要を追い風に景気は回復
韓国景気は回復している。実質GDPは 1～3月
期に大きく増加した後、4～6月期も前期比＋0.6％
とプラス成長を維持した（右上図）。
外需が景気回復をけん引している。AI利用の拡
大、とりわけ推論需要の増加に伴い、韓国企業が
強みを持つメモリ半導体への需要が急増している。
これを背景に、輸出額は過去最高水準で推移して
いる。
個人消費は良好な所得環境や政府の支援策に支
えられ、緩やかに増加している。大手企業では、
高水準の賃上げやボーナスの増加を背景に、上半
期の平均給与が前年同期比＋ 7.1％と大きく増加
した。一方、雇用の多くを占める中小企業では、
賃金がほとんど増加しておらず、所得格差が拡大
している。こうしたなか、政府は中東情勢悪化に
よる物価高の影響を和らげるため、4月から所得下
位 7 割を対象に最大約6 万円相当の支援金を給付

した。これにより中低所得層の購買力が下支えさ
れ、中東危機による消費への悪影響が緩和された。
設備投資も増加している。AI需要の拡大を背景に、半導体関連産業で設備投資が活発化してい
ることに加え、研究開発やソフトウェア投資も増加している。
■個人消費の減速が景気の重石に
年後半も韓国経済は回復基調を維持し、 今年の実質GDP成長率は＋3.2％に達すると予想する。
AI投資の収益性を巡る懸念は根強いものの、 海外のデータセンター投資は引き続き拡大しており、
半導体を中心としたAI関連財の輸出は高水準を維持する見込みである。
もっとも、個人消費は徐々に減速する見通しである。政府による支援金の押し上げ効果がはく
落することに加え、エネルギー価格の高騰が幅広い財・サービス価格に転嫁され、家計の購買力
が圧迫されると予想する。
足元の消費者物価は前年比＋3％前後と、金融政策運営上の物価目標である＋2％を上回ってい
る。エネルギー価格の高止まりに加え、パソコンやスマートフォンなど半導体搭載製品の価格が

上昇していることが背景にある。インフレ抑制に向けて、韓国銀行は7 月から 2 カ月連続で政策
金利を 0.25％ポイントずつ引き上げ、3.0％とした。
反面、急速な利上げで景気が下押しされるリスクもある。韓国では諸外国と比べて家計債務残
高が大きく、変動金利型ローンの割合も高い（右下図）。金利上昇による利払い負担の増加は、
家計の可処分所得を減少させ、個人消費を下押しする恐れがある。さらに、資金調達の難化や建
設コストの上昇を通じて、建設不況が長期化する可能性もある。実際、建設会社の廃業申請数は
前年から 1割以上増加している。
研究員 今川 冬馬（imaga wa.thoma@jri.co.jp）
